Annals definitions

Kevin Buzzard compiled a list of 100 definitions that Mathlib lacks and that are needed to state the main theorems of Annals of Mathematics papers from 2020 to mid-2026 (see the Zulip thread Roadmaps for Annals theorem statements). Of the 207 papers, about 50 can already be stated importing only Mathlib. With these 100 definitions, he estimates that about 50 more could be stated and about 60 more partly stated.

This repository tracks where each of those definitions stands with respect to Tau Ceti and its roadmaps. It is not a roadmap: roadmaps remain the only specifications of work, and nothing here asks for a definition to be built outside the theory it belongs to.

Snapshot 2026-11-25 (Tau Ceti b22b77af44c, Mathlib 31ce06eaadf4, roadmaps 0e620b7de, 391 open roadmap PRs). This page is generated from the files of github.com/TauCetiProject/AnnalsDefinitions, which hold the evidence for every entry.

Status

Status of each definition against Mathlib, Tau Ceti code, merged roadmaps and open roadmap PRs. See README.md for sources, the snapshot this describes, and how to update it. Each row links to its evidence file in census/.

StatusMeaningCount
The definition exists in Tau Ceti code, adequately for the cited papers.5
A special case or close variant exists in code, but not what the papers need.18
A merged roadmap specifies it as a target; not (fully) implemented.58
Only an open roadmap PR specifies it.19
Not a target anywhere, but its main prerequisites are targets or already exist.0
Neither it nor its main prerequisites are planned.0

No definition is in Mathlib in the generality the papers need.

By definition

Algebra, representation theory & homotopy theory

#DefinitionStatusWhere
1Real rank of a semisimple Lie groupcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeRealSemisimpleLieGroups §1.3, §2.1, §2.4 (§0.1 Cartan involutions, §1.1 maximal abelian subspaces of 𝔭 and §1.2's restricted root space decomposition in code); inputs LieGroups L7, L9
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • RealSemisimpleLieGroups (merged): Real semisimple Lie groups, parabolic subgroups and Riemannian symmetric spaces. Builds the structure theory of real semisimple and reductive Lie groups on top of the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions: Cartan involutions, restricted roots, the real rank, the restricted Weyl group, minimal and general parabolic subgroups with their Langlands decompositions, and the Bruhat decomposition. It then develops the Riemannian symmetric space G/K: its invariant metric, curvature and geodesics, flats and rank, the conjugacy of maximal compact subgroups, and quotients by discrete subgroups.
  • RepresentationTheory/LieGroups (merged): Lie groups and the Lie algebra correspondence. Builds the correspondence between Lie groups and Lie algebras: the exponential map, the adjoint representation, the closed-subgroup theorem, the Lie functor and Lie's third theorem. It continues with the structure of compact and reductive groups: maximal tori and the Weyl integration formula, complexification and real forms, the Borel–Weil theorem, and the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions with restricted roots.

Also cited in its census file: ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups, LieGroupsPartII, NoncompactQuantumSymmetry, ReductiveGroupsPartIII.

2p-block (Brauer block) of a finite groupcensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: block idempotents IsBlockIdempotent of R[G] with the block decomposition, scalar extension and lifting of blocks from k[G] to O[G]; ModularCharactersAndBlocks §§1.1, 1.3 (splitting p-modular system, lifting projectives; lifting idempotents in code), §§4.2–4.3 (Irr(B), IBr(B), SameBlock, principal block; SameBlock on complex characters and the principal block idempotent in code); consumed by ModularCharactersAndBlocksPartII
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • RepresentationTheory/ModularCharactersAndBlocks (merged): Modular characters and blocks of finite groups. Develops the modular representation theory of a finite group at a prime p: p-modular systems, Brauer characters and the decomposition map, projective characters and Brauer reciprocity, blocks over the three coefficient rings, the Brauer homomorphism and defect groups. Its main results are Brauer's three main theorems, the conjugacy of defect groups, and the behaviour of blocks under normal subgroups.
  • ModularCharactersAndBlocksPartII (merged): Modular characters and blocks, Part II — the Glauberman correspondence and heights of characters. Continues the theory of blocks in two directions. The first is the character theory of coprime actions, ending in the Glauberman correspondence and its degree divisibility. The second is the theory of heights: Brauer's theorem on heights, height zero characters, the Fong–Reynolds correspondence and Reynolds' theorem on blocks with a normal defect group, with the height zero and Alperin–McKay conjectures stated as properties of blocks.

Also cited in its census file: RiemannHypothesisForDirichletLFunctions/FiniteReductiveGroups, GlaubermanZStar.

4Parabolic subgroups of a real semisimple Lie group (minimal and standard parabolics)census filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: algebraic parabolics HopfIdeal.IsParabolic, Cocharacter.parabolic, dynamic parabolics of real matrix groups Subgroup.dynamicParabolic; Lie-group parabolics in RealSemisimpleLieGroups §3.1, §§4.1–4.5, P = MAN also in AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §2.3a and, for every reductive Cartan datum, LieGroupsPartII Layer 3
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • RealSemisimpleLieGroups (merged): Real semisimple Lie groups, parabolic subgroups and Riemannian symmetric spaces. Builds the structure theory of real semisimple and reductive Lie groups on top of the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions: Cartan involutions, restricted roots, the real rank, the restricted Weyl group, minimal and general parabolic subgroups with their Langlands decompositions, and the Bruhat decomposition. It then develops the Riemannian symmetric space G/K: its invariant metric, curvature and geodesics, flats and rank, the conjugacy of maximal compact subgroups, and quotients by discrete subgroups.
  • AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups (merged): Automorphic forms on reductive groups. Builds the analytic and representation-theoretic setting for automorphic forms on a connected reductive group over a number field: smooth, Schwartz and moderate-growth functions on the adelic group, Harish-Chandra modules of the real group with infinitesimal characters, discrete series and archimedean Langlands parameters, automorphic representations with their local components, and constant terms and cuspidality. It ends with cohomological and coherent representations and with explicit dictionaries for GL₁, GL₂ and GSp₄, including the passage from classical modular forms to adelic ones.
  • LieGroupsPartII (merged): Lie groups, part II — minimal parabolic subgroups, the Bruhat decomposition and the self-adjoint matrix model of real reductive groups. Continues the structure theory of real reductive Lie groups after the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions, for groups that need not be connected, semisimple, linear or of finite centre. It proves conjugacy of Cartan involutions and of Iwasawa decompositions with a common split centre, defines the minimal parabolic subgroup with its Langlands decomposition, proves the Bruhat decomposition, and shows that the real points of a connected reductive group over ℝ, realised as a self-adjoint matrix group, carry all of this structure.

Also cited in its census file: ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactorsPartII, NoncompactQuantumSymmetry.

5Infinitesimal character (and Casimir eigenvalue) of a representation of a real reductive groupcensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: harishChandraIso, centralCharacterModules, UniversalEnvelopingAlgebra.centralCharacter (semisimple, alg. closed), HasInfinitesimalCharacter on Lie modules, smooth vectors lieSmoothVectors; reductive HC in LieHighestWeight §9.2; group level in AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §2.2
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • RepresentationTheory/LieHighestWeight (merged): Representations of semisimple Lie algebras, highest weight theory, and the Weyl formulas. Develops complements to the highest weight theory of semisimple Lie algebras, building on Verma modules, the Weyl character formula and the Harish-Chandra isomorphism. It proves that Serre's relations present a split semisimple Lie algebra in characteristic zero, constructs explicit models of the exceptional Lie algebras, among them G₂ and F₄ as derivation algebras of the octonions and of the Albert algebra, extends the comparisons of centres of enveloping algebras to reductive Lie algebras such as gl(n), and studies the affine Weyl group acting on the closed fundamental alcove.
  • AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups (merged): Automorphic forms on reductive groups. Builds the analytic and representation-theoretic setting for automorphic forms on a connected reductive group over a number field: smooth, Schwartz and moderate-growth functions on the adelic group, Harish-Chandra modules of the real group with infinitesimal characters, discrete series and archimedean Langlands parameters, automorphic representations with their local components, and constant terms and cuspidality. It ends with cohomological and coherent representations and with explicit dictionaries for GL₁, GL₂ and GSp₄, including the passage from classical modular forms to adelic ones.

Also cited in its census file: AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII, AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors, AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactorsPartII, AutomorphicSpectralTheory, EndoscopicTransferAndUnitaryTraceComparison, IgusaVarietiesAndTorsionConcentrationPartII, QuantitativeOrbitMethodAndMicrolocalAnalysisOnLieGroups.

6Convergence of a spectral sequencecensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: spectral sequence of a filtered complex, convergence for finite, exhaustive, bounded-below and complete filtrations; bidegree assignments (§2.1) and the towers of §§1.2, 4.1; general convergence in SpectralSequencesAndConvergence §§1.4, 3.2, 3.4, 4.4
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • SpectralSequencesAndConvergence (merged): Spectral sequences and convergence. Builds, over an arbitrary abelian category, the language in which a spectral sequence converges: cycle and boundary towers, the limit page, and weak, ordinary and strong convergence to filtered objects in a prescribed set of degrees. It proves the comparison theorem, convergence for filtered complexes under the usual boundedness hypotheses, and Boardman's theorems on conditional convergence of exact couples.

Also cited in its census file: SpectralSequencesAndConvergencePartII.

7Discrete series representations of GL2(ℝ)census filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: D_k(μ) as an explicit 𝔤𝔩₂(ℂ)-module (CompactWeightModule.discreteSeries), without the O(2)-action; AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §§2.1–2.6 (D_k(μ) in §2.6); GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R16.2.13
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups (merged): Automorphic forms on reductive groups. Builds the analytic and representation-theoretic setting for automorphic forms on a connected reductive group over a number field: smooth, Schwartz and moderate-growth functions on the adelic group, Harish-Chandra modules of the real group with infinitesimal characters, discrete series and archimedean Langlands parameters, automorphic representations with their local components, and constant terms and cuspidality. It ends with cohomological and coherent representations and with explicit dictionaries for GL₁, GL₂ and GSp₄, including the passage from classical modular forms to adelic ones.
  • GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer (merged): GL₂ automorphic representations and transfer. Builds the local and adelic representation theory of GL₂ that links classical modular forms to automorphic transfer: newvectors, Whittaker functions and local factors, the comparison with classical and Hilbert modular forms, the Jacquet–Langlands correspondence, and cyclic and solvable base change. Its applications include the automorphy of solvable Artin representations and modularity theorems in characteristic two.

Also cited in its census file: ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpacesPartII, AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors, AutomorphicSpectralTheory, EndoscopicTransferAndUnitaryTraceComparison, HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves.

10Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg antisymmetrisation mapcensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2447 §§7.4–7.5 (polyvector fields, detCochain, Φ^n_HKR), §9.2 (cohomological_hkr); Hochschild cochain complex and HHⁿ(A, M) in code; DGAInfinity L8
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • HochschildHomologyAndHKR (open PR #2447): Hochschild homology and the Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg theorem. Builds Hochschild homology and cohomology of an associative algebra over a commutative ring with coefficients in a bimodule: the complexes and their normalisation, the low degrees, the bar resolution with the comparison to Ext and Tor, and the cup product. Its main result is the cohomological Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg theorem, which identifies the Hochschild cohomology of an algebra that is smooth and projective over the base ring with its polyvector fields; polynomial algebras are computed by the Koszul resolution.
  • DGAInfinity (merged): DG and A-infinity algebras, categories, and modules. Builds the algebraic foundations of differential graded and A∞ algebras, categories, modules and bimodules, from signed multilinear algebra through bar–cobar constructions and Maurer–Cartan twisting. Main results include Kadeishvili's minimal model theorem with homological transfer, derived Morita theory with compact generators, Drinfeld's DG quotients and their comparison with Verdier quotients, Hochschild deformation theory and intrinsic formality, smooth, proper and Calabi–Yau structures, Euler forms on perfect objects, and relative quadratic Koszul duality.

Also cited in its census file: EulerSystemsAndKolyvaginSystems, KTheoryFiniteLocalFields, RefinedTraceMethods.

11Split classical groups Sp2n, SOl and general spin groups GSpinlcensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: Sp₂ₘ pinned group scheme, SOₙ of the identity form, split SO_l of J_l with its diagonal torus, reductive over fields of characteristic not two, the Clifford torus of hyperbolic pairs, its upper triangular and root subgroups, J_{Sp₂ₙ} with its pinning, GSpin on points, the root data of Def. 11.2 and its dualities, C₀(q₃) ≅ M₂, C₀(q₄) ≅ M₂ × M₂, the orthogonal group scheme O(q); ReductiveGroups §9B.5 (Sp₂ₙ, GSp₂ₙ), §9B.6 (SO(q), split SO_l of J_l), §9B.7 (GSpin, Spin, root datum of Def. 11.2), §9B.8 (low rank, dual root data); open Tau Ceti PRs for GSp₂ₘ, GPin, GSpin on points over a ring and smoothness of the Borel of SO_l^J
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ReductiveGroups (merged): Reductive algebraic groups. Builds the general theory of affine algebraic groups and reductive groups from Hopf algebras and comodules: representations, Lie algebras, quotients, tori, solvable and unipotent groups, structure theory with root data, parabolics and the Bruhat decomposition, finite groups of Lie type, and classification by root data. It constructs the pinned Chevalley–Demazure group scheme over the integers of every root datum, and identifies with these models the classical group schemes over every commutative ring: symplectic and symplectic-similitude groups, orthogonal and special orthogonal groups of a quadratic form with the Dickson invariant, and general spin and spin groups, with their low-rank isomorphisms. It also develops highest-weight representations and regular-semisimple spectral quotients.

Also cited in its census file: AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactorsPartII, EndoscopicTransferAndUnitaryTraceComparison, MetaplecticAutomorphicForms, TwistedAutomorphicDescents.

Algebraic geometry

#DefinitionStatusWhere
12The Hodge structure on H1 of a complex abelian varietycensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: weight-one Hodge structure of a polarized lattice (latticeHodgeStructure), compact connected commutative complex Lie groups as complex tori (mulInvariantExpQuotientKerDiffeomorph); H¹(A) in AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli §§A5.1–A5.5, A5.7; analytification in ComplexComparisonPartII §C-1.1
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli (merged): Abelian schemes and arithmetic moduli (the Jacobian challenge, part II). Develops abelian schemes over an arbitrary base: rigidity and the theorem of the cube in families, the dual abelian scheme, polarizations, isogenies and Weil pairings, Tate modules with the de Rham and crystalline realizations, and the deformation theorems of Serre–Tate and Grothendieck–Messing. Over the complex numbers it gives the lattice uniformization, Riemann forms, the Appell–Humbert theorem and the equivalence between polarized abelian varieties and polarized Hodge structures of weight one. Over a field it proves the basic arithmetic of homomorphisms, including finiteness of Hom and positivity of the Rosati involution.
  • ComplexComparisonPartII (merged): Complex comparison, part II — coherent, de Rham–Betti and étale comparison. Compares complex algebraic geometry with complex analytic geometry and topology for coherent, de Rham and étale coefficients. Main results are coherent GAGA for projective and proper schemes and derived GAGA for proper algebraic spaces, Remmert's proper mapping theorem and Chow's theorem, the comparison of algebraic de Rham and singular cohomology with Gauss–Manin connections and Hodge theory, Deligne's Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for regular singular connections, the Riemann existence theorem, and Artin's comparison of étale and singular cohomology with finite and adic coefficients.

Also cited in its census file: ReductiveGroupsPartIII, SatoTateGroups, ShimuraData.

13Mumford–Tate group of a rational Hodge structurecensus fileIn Tau CetiTask file: statementsCode: h_V and MT(V) of a rational Hodge structure (HodgeStructureOn.deligneHom, HodgeStructureOn.mumfordTateHopfIdeal), on MT(h) of an algebraic h : S → G_ℝ (TauCeti.DeligneTorus.mumfordTateHopfIdeal, mumfordTateClosedSubgroup) and its geometric connectedness (Prop. 13.3(c)); the properties of Prop. 13.3(d) in ShimuraVarieties §P.3a; also ShimuraData §§1.1–1.4
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ShimuraVarieties (merged): Complex Shimura varieties and canonical models. Constructs complex Shimura varieties as towers of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains, their Baily–Borel compactifications, and canonical models over reflex fields: first the Siegel case by complex multiplication, then Hodge and abelian type, then general Shimura data. It also treats Mumford–Tate groups and the Galois representations of abelian varieties with values in them.
  • ShimuraData (merged): Shimura data, Hermitian domains, and adelic level structures. Builds the Hodge-theoretic and group-theoretic material used with Shimura data: rational weights, Mumford–Tate groups and Hodge-generic abelian varieties, variations of Hodge structure and their polarizations, compact duals over reflex fields, and explicit Hilbert and Siegel data. Its main results are that every connected Shimura datum lifts to a full datum satisfying the additional axioms, and a neatness criterion for certain adelic level subgroups.

Also cited in its census file: ReductiveGroupsPartIII, SatoTateGroups, MordellLawrenceVenkateshPartII, ShimuraVarietiesPartII.

14The volume form ωn and integration on a compact Kähler (or Hermitian) manifoldcensus fileIn Tau CetiTask file: statementsCode: SmoothTwoForm.volumeForm, liouvilleMeasure, IsKahler (Geometry/Symplectic/Manifold/{Volume,LiouvilleMeasure,Kahler}.lean); comparison dvol = ωⁿ/n! in ComplexKahlerGeometry §5.9 and ComplexHessianEquations §3.2
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ComplexKahlerGeometry (merged): Complex manifolds, Dolbeault theory and Kähler geometry. Develops complex manifolds, Dolbeault theory and Kähler geometry: the Newlander–Nirenberg theorem, the Dolbeault–Grothendieck lemma, the local algebra of holomorphic germs, holomorphic and Hermitian bundles, and elliptic theory on compact Hermitian manifolds with Serre duality. On compact Kähler manifolds it proves the Kähler identities, the Hodge decomposition, hard Lefschetz and the Hodge–Riemann relations.
  • ComplexHessianEquations (merged): Complex Hessian equations. Treats fully nonlinear elliptic equations of complex Hessian type on compact complex manifolds, written through the eigenvalues of a real (1,1)-form relative to a Hermitian metric. It develops spectral functions, Gårding cones and admissible pairs, the comparison principle and uniqueness, and on compact Kähler manifolds Yau's theorem on the complex Monge–Ampère equation and the sharp L∞ estimates of Guo, Phong and Tong.

Also cited in its census file: CalibratedGeometry, #1854, #2536.

15Ball quotients as quasi-projective varietiescensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeBallQuotientsAndComplexHyperbolicLattices §§7.1, 9.5, 10.4 (all lattices, Mok compactification), §11.4 (Baily–Borel); PU(p,q) and the Siegel model of the ball in code; ball and PU(1,n) in RealSemisimpleLieGroups §5.7; analytification ComplexComparisonPartII; arithmetic case ShimuraVarieties §2.7
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • BallQuotientsAndComplexHyperbolicLattices (merged): Ball quotients and complex hyperbolic lattices. Studies the complex ball as the symmetric space of PU(1,n) and the lattices in this group, arithmetic or not: cusps, finite generation, Selberg's lemma, and the quotient of the ball as a normal complex space. It constructs the toroidal and minimal compactifications and proves that the minimal compactification is a normal projective variety, so that every ball quotient is quasi-projective, and that the quotient by a torsion-free lattice has a unique algebraic structure; in the arithmetic case it compares with the Baily–Borel compactification. It ends with totally geodesic and special subvarieties.
  • RealSemisimpleLieGroups (merged): Real semisimple Lie groups, parabolic subgroups and Riemannian symmetric spaces. Builds the structure theory of real semisimple and reductive Lie groups on top of the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions: Cartan involutions, restricted roots, the real rank, the restricted Weyl group, minimal and general parabolic subgroups with their Langlands decompositions, and the Bruhat decomposition. It then develops the Riemannian symmetric space G/K: its invariant metric, curvature and geodesics, flats and rank, the conjugacy of maximal compact subgroups, and quotients by discrete subgroups.
  • ComplexComparisonPartII (merged): Complex comparison, part II — coherent, de Rham–Betti and étale comparison. Compares complex algebraic geometry with complex analytic geometry and topology for coherent, de Rham and étale coefficients. Main results are coherent GAGA for projective and proper schemes and derived GAGA for proper algebraic spaces, Remmert's proper mapping theorem and Chow's theorem, the comparison of algebraic de Rham and singular cohomology with Gauss–Manin connections and Hodge theory, Deligne's Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for regular singular connections, the Riemann existence theorem, and Artin's comparison of étale and singular cohomology with finite and adic coefficients.
  • ShimuraVarieties (merged): Complex Shimura varieties and canonical models. Constructs complex Shimura varieties as towers of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains, their Baily–Borel compactifications, and canonical models over reflex fields: first the Siegel case by complex multiplication, then Hodge and abelian type, then general Shimura data. It also treats Mumford–Tate groups and the Galois representations of abelian varieties with values in them.

Also cited in its census file: AdelicAlgebraicGroups, ArithmeticFuchsianGroups, ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, ShimuraCompactifications, ShimuraData.

16Rational Chow groups of a Deligne–Mumford stack and the degree of zero-cyclescensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: reviewVirtualFundamentalClass §§2.1–2.2, 7.7 (rational Chow groups of separated DM stacks, degree on proper stacks); integral closed substacks (16.1), non-separated stacks, 16.4(b) and the zero-cycle degree on every stack in PR #2446 §§1.0, 2.2, 5.2, 5.4
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • VirtualFundamentalClass (merged): The Behrend–Fantechi virtual fundamental class. Carries out the construction of Behrend and Fantechi: the intrinsic normal cone of a Deligne–Mumford stack, obstruction theories, and the virtual fundamental class of a perfect obstruction theory with a global resolution, shown to be independent of all choices. Along the way it builds rational Chow groups, Gysin maps and degrees of zero-cycles for separated Deligne–Mumford stacks, and a relative version of the class.
  • VirtualFundamentalClassPartII (open PR #2446): Virtual fundamental classes, part II: integral and non-separated Chow theory of Deligne–Mumford stacks and virtual classes over general bases. Extends the Chow theory of Deligne–Mumford stacks to integral coefficients and to stacks that need not be separated, with the intersection ring of a smooth stack, the comparison with the moduli space, and the degree of the residual gerbe at a closed point. It defines the virtual class without the resolution property, proves deformation invariance of virtual counts, and constructs the perfect obstruction theory of spaces of maps from curves.

Also cited in its census file: GromovWittenTheory, StackPointsAndResidualGerbes, AlgebraicGromovWittenTheory.

17Cohomologically affine morphisms of algebraic stackscensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2489 §1.22a (Stacks.IsCohomologicallyAffine, Groups.IsLinearlyReductive); on SchemeAndStackFoundations §1.22 (QCoh.pushforward); linear reductivity in code
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • #2489 (pull request): SchemeAndStackFoundations: cohomologically affine morphisms. Extends the merged roadmap SchemeAndStackFoundations with a section on cohomologically affine morphisms of algebraic stacks (quasi-compact, quasi-separated, with exact quasi-coherent pushforward) and linearly reductive group schemes over a base, with flat base change, Serre's affineness criterion and the comparison with complete reducibility of comodules over a field; it adds no roadmap.
  • SchemeAndStackFoundations (merged): Scheme, stack, cohomology and intersection foundations. Collects the general algebraic geometry on which arithmetic geometry rests. It covers descent, algebraic spaces, torsors and algebraic stacks, and cohomology on the Zariski, étale, fppf and pro-étale sites with coherent duality. It also treats curves, divisors and Picard functors, deformation theory, formal schemes and alterations, and Chow groups with Chern classes and Riemann–Roch.

Also cited in its census file: AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry, StackPointsAndResidualGerbes, QuasicoherentSheavesOnPrestacks.

18Cup product in étale cohomologycensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeEtaleCohomologyFoundations §F.1, §0.9 (ShiftedHom cup product; schemes); rigid case in GaloisActionOnEtaleCohomology §1.1; Čech cup product of a family (cechCup, cechCupHomology, graded commutative) and over a directed system of families (cechCohomologyCup) in code
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • EtaleCohomologyFoundations (merged): Étale cohomology foundations. Builds étale cohomology of schemes with finite, adic and rational coefficients: Nagata compactification, constructible sheaves, proper and smooth base change, cohomological dimension, Artin vanishing and finiteness, purity, duality and perverse sheaves. Its main results are the six operations on bounded constructible complexes with their base change, Künneth and duality theorems, and the exact sequence of arithmetic fundamental groups.
  • GaloisActionOnEtaleCohomology (merged): The Galois action on étale cohomology of geometric fibres. Constructs the action of the absolute Galois group on the étale cohomology of the geometric fibre, for a scheme over a field and for a rigid space over a complete non-archimedean field. It proves the functoriality of the action, its continuity with ℓ-adic and rational coefficients, the comparison with higher direct images to the base point, and the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence.

Also cited in its census file: ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology, EtaleDualityAndPerverseSheaves, ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomologyPartII.

19Enriques surface over an algebraically closed fieldcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeWeilConjectures §7.12 (Enriques condition over any field); characteristic ≠ 2 theory in EnriquesSurfaces; characteristic-free theory in EnriquesSurfacesAndIntegralNonexistence Layer 0
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • WeilConjectures (merged): Weil conjectures and cohomological zeta functions. Relates point counts over finite fields to the zeta function and its cohomological factors, and assembles the Weil conjectures for a smooth proper scheme over a finite field: integral factors independent of ℓ, the absolute values of the reciprocal roots, and the functional equation. It also contains explicit calculations for curves and surfaces, among them the definition and numerical invariants of Enriques surfaces.
  • EnriquesSurfaces (merged): Enriques surfaces, projective models and automorphisms. Constructs the algebraic geometry of Enriques surfaces: integral Picard and numerical invariants, genus-one pencils, linear systems of low degree, double-plane and Reye models, and automorphism groups through their action on the associated lattices. Its main results identify the automorphism group of a generic nodal or unnodal Enriques surface, away from small characteristics, with a level-two congruence subgroup of a reflection group acting on such a lattice.
  • EnriquesSurfacesAndIntegralNonexistence (merged): Enriques surfaces and nonexistence over the integers. Treats the arithmetic of Enriques surfaces in all characteristics, including two: genus-one fibrations, canonical coverings, families and Picard descent. Its main results are that there is no flat proper family of Enriques surfaces over the integers, and that there is no non-exceptional Enriques surface over the field with two elements whose Picard scheme is constant.

Also cited in its census file: K3Surfaces, K3SurfacesAndSymplecticBoundedness, MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles.

21Fppf cohomology with supports in a closed subsetcensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2531 §2.9a (Scheme.fppfCohomologyWithSupport); PR #1840 §0.2; finite flat groups in FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory; site-general Sheaf.relativeH' in Tau Ceti (not at the universe of the big fppf site)
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • #2531 (pull request): SchemeAndStackFoundations: quasi-affine descent, quasi-finite spaces and fppf cohomology with supports. Extends the merged roadmap SchemeAndStackFoundations with effective fppf descent of affine, quasi-affine and separated locally quasi-finite morphisms, separated locally quasi-finite algebraic spaces over a scheme, and cohomology on the big fppf site with coefficients in the larger universe, including fppf cohomology with supports in a closed subset with its long exact sequence and excision; it adds no roadmap.
  • PurityForFlatCohomology (open PR #1840): Purity for flat cohomology and the conjectures of Gabber. Proves purity for flat cohomology after Česnavičius and Scholze: for a Noetherian local complete intersection and a commutative finite flat group scheme, the fppf cohomology with supports in the closed point vanishes below the dimension. It builds fppf cohomology with supports, and deduces purity for torsors and gerbes, Gabber's conjectures on local Picard and Brauer groups, and purity for the Brauer group of regular schemes.
  • FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory (merged): Finite flat group schemes and integral p-adic Hodge theory. Develops finite flat commutative group schemes and p-divisible groups, with the integral p-adic Hodge theory classifying them by semilinear algebra: Tate's Hodge–Tate decomposition and generic fibre theorem, Raynaud's results and the Oort–Tate classification, Dieudonné theory with the Dieudonné–Manin classification, Fontaine–Laffaille theory, and Breuil–Kisin theory, including Kisin's classification of crystalline lattices and of p-divisible groups over p-adic integer rings. Applications include local residual types and Serre weights, Fontaine's ramification bound, flat deformation conditions and Raynaud's determinant theorem.

Also cited in its census file: FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheoryPartII, SchemeAndStackFoundationsPartII.

22Galois action on the étale cohomology of the geometric fibrecensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeGaloisActionOnEtaleCohomology Layers 0–3 (rigid action ρ(σ), dictionary with R^i f_*); scheme case EtaleCohomologyFoundations §F.4.6; ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology §1.1
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • GaloisActionOnEtaleCohomology (merged): The Galois action on étale cohomology of geometric fibres. Constructs the action of the absolute Galois group on the étale cohomology of the geometric fibre, for a scheme over a field and for a rigid space over a complete non-archimedean field. It proves the functoriality of the action, its continuity with ℓ-adic and rational coefficients, the comparison with higher direct images to the base point, and the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence.
  • EtaleCohomologyFoundations (merged): Étale cohomology foundations. Builds étale cohomology of schemes with finite, adic and rational coefficients: Nagata compactification, constructible sheaves, proper and smooth base change, cohomological dimension, Artin vanishing and finiteness, purity, duality and perverse sheaves. Its main results are the six operations on bounded constructible complexes with their base change, Künneth and duality theorems, and the exact sequence of arithmetic fundamental groups.
  • ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology (merged): Classical analytic étale cohomology. Develops the étale cohomology of analytic adic spaces needed to pass from schemes and formal models to analytic spaces, starting from sheaves with supports, henselian comparison, formal specialization and analytic covering spaces. Main results are comparison and invariance theorems for nearby cycles over valuation rings, cohomology with proper supports, normalized traces for smooth morphisms and Poincaré duality, comparison with Berkovich spaces, and computations for discs, annuli, exhaustions of affine space and the boundary of a smooth curve.

Also cited in its census file: CohomologyComparisons, DiamondEtaleCohomology, PadicDifferentialEquationsAndRigidCohomology.

23Hodge-generic abelian varietycensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeShimuraData §1.5 hodgeGeneric, on §1.2; MT also in ShimuraVarieties §P.3a (Mumford–Tate Hopf ideal of h and of a rational Hodge structure in code); H¹(A) in AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli §A5
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ShimuraData (merged): Shimura data, Hermitian domains, and adelic level structures. Builds the Hodge-theoretic and group-theoretic material used with Shimura data: rational weights, Mumford–Tate groups and Hodge-generic abelian varieties, variations of Hodge structure and their polarizations, compact duals over reflex fields, and explicit Hilbert and Siegel data. Its main results are that every connected Shimura datum lifts to a full datum satisfying the additional axioms, and a neatness criterion for certain adelic level subgroups.
  • ShimuraVarieties (merged): Complex Shimura varieties and canonical models. Constructs complex Shimura varieties as towers of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains, their Baily–Borel compactifications, and canonical models over reflex fields: first the Siegel case by complex multiplication, then Hodge and abelian type, then general Shimura data. It also treats Mumford–Tate groups and the Galois representations of abelian varieties with values in them.
  • AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli (merged): Abelian schemes and arithmetic moduli (the Jacobian challenge, part II). Develops abelian schemes over an arbitrary base: rigidity and the theorem of the cube in families, the dual abelian scheme, polarizations, isogenies and Weil pairings, Tate modules with the de Rham and crystalline realizations, and the deformation theorems of Serre–Tate and Grothendieck–Messing. Over the complex numbers it gives the lattice uniformization, Riemann forms, the Appell–Humbert theorem and the equivalence between polarized abelian varieties and polarized Hodge structures of weight one. Over a field it proves the basic arithmetic of homomorphisms, including finiteness of Hom and positivity of the Rosati involution.

Also cited in its census file: AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliPartII, PELModuli, ReductiveGroupsPartIII, SatoTateGroups, MordellLawrenceVenkateshPartII, ShimuraVarietiesPartII.

24Moduli stack of stable maps ℳg,n(X,β)census fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #1998 Layer 3 MbarMap (on merged StableReductionPartII §§3.1–3.3, 3.11); StableReduction L10–11 (single stable maps; IsStableMap in code)
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AlgebraicGromovWittenTheory (open PR #1998): Algebraic Gromov–Witten theory and quantum cohomology. Constructs algebraic Gromov–Witten theory for a smooth projective variety over a field of characteristic zero: the moduli stack of stable maps, its virtual fundamental class, and primary and descendant invariants with the string, dilaton and divisor equations. From the genus-zero invariants it builds small and big quantum cohomology and proves associativity through the WDVV equation.
  • StableReductionPartII (merged): Stable reduction of curves, Part II. Develops, building on stable reduction over discrete valuation rings, the explicit geometry of moduli of stable pointed curves over arbitrary bases: tricanonical embeddings, local charts at nodes, adding and contracting markings, Hodge bundles, stable compactifications, Torelli maps and relative Picard functors. Main results identify the universal curve with the moduli of curves with one extra marking, give the integral Noether isomorphism of determinant lines, extend families after finite base change, and prove positivity of the Hodge bundle under maximal variation.
  • StableReduction (merged): Stable reduction of curves and stable maps. Proves stable reduction for curves over a discrete valuation ring: after a finite extension of the base, a stable model exists and is unique. It also defines stable maps to a scheme and their families, with the stability criteria and the stabilization construction that a proof of properness of the moduli space of stable maps requires; the moduli stack itself is not constructed here.

Also cited in its census file: AnalyticStableMaps, GromovWittenTheory, ImplicitAtlases, VirtualFundamentalClassPartII.

25Nash blowup of a varietycensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeNashBlowups §§1.2, 2.1–2.7 (nashBlowup); AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry §1.3 (coherentGrassmannian)
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • NashBlowups (merged): Nash blowups. Constructs the Nash blowup of a variety as the closure of the smooth locus in the Grassmannian of rank-d quotients of the sheaf of differentials, together with the normalized Nash blowup and the iterations of both. It proves the universal property, Nobile's theorem that in characteristic zero the Nash blowup is an isomorphism only for smooth varieties, the theorem of Duarte and Núñez-Betancourt for normal varieties in characteristic p, and a principle by which iterated Nash blowups never resolve certain singularities.
  • AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry (merged): Algebraic moduli and representability for arithmetic geometry. Builds the representability machinery for moduli problems: projective bundles, Grassmannians of quotients of coherent sheaves and flag schemes, Hilbert, Hom and Isom schemes, effective descent, gerbes, rigidification of stacks, and Artin's representability criteria. Main results include Mumford's uniform regularity bound and the boundedness of Hilbert polynomials, the classification of gerbes with abelian band by their class in H², Artin approximation, and nonarchimedean GAGA for proper algebraic spaces.

30Theta divisor of a Jacobiancensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeJacobianChallenge §§C.4, D.5, E.5; θ_α and relative Θ in JacobianChallengePartII §§3.2–3.3, 3.10–3.13
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • JacobianChallenge (merged): The Jacobian challenge. For a smooth proper geometrically connected curve over a field, builds the Jacobian as an abelian variety representing the degree-zero Picard functor, without assuming a rational point, and, for each rational point, the Abel–Jacobi map with its universal property. Along the way it develops Riemann–Roch and Serre duality on curves, relative Picard schemes, and abelian varieties with their duals, polarizations and theta divisor.
  • JacobianChallengePartII (merged): The Jacobian challenge, Part II — relative Jacobians, theta bundles and universal differences. Continues the Jacobian challenge in families: for a smooth projective curve over a noetherian base it shows that the relative Jacobian is an abelian scheme with a canonical principal polarization, and builds Abel maps without a chosen section. It then treats theta divisors and the Poincaré bundle for an arbitrary divisor of degree one, and the difference and Faltings–Zhang morphisms on fibre powers.

31Totally geodesic subvariety of a ball quotientcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeBallQuotientsAndComplexHyperbolicLattices §§12.1–12.3 (Γ-special and totally geodesic subvarieties), §3.3; ball model and Proposition 31.3 in RealSemisimpleLieGroups §5.7; RiemannianGeometry R5a
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • BallQuotientsAndComplexHyperbolicLattices (merged): Ball quotients and complex hyperbolic lattices. Studies the complex ball as the symmetric space of PU(1,n) and the lattices in this group, arithmetic or not: cusps, finite generation, Selberg's lemma, and the quotient of the ball as a normal complex space. It constructs the toroidal and minimal compactifications and proves that the minimal compactification is a normal projective variety, so that every ball quotient is quasi-projective, and that the quotient by a torsion-free lattice has a unique algebraic structure; in the arithmetic case it compares with the Baily–Borel compactification. It ends with totally geodesic and special subvarieties.
  • RealSemisimpleLieGroups (merged): Real semisimple Lie groups, parabolic subgroups and Riemannian symmetric spaces. Builds the structure theory of real semisimple and reductive Lie groups on top of the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions: Cartan involutions, restricted roots, the real rank, the restricted Weyl group, minimal and general parabolic subgroups with their Langlands decompositions, and the Bruhat decomposition. It then develops the Riemannian symmetric space G/K: its invariant metric, curvature and geodesics, flats and rank, the conjugacy of maximal compact subgroups, and quotients by discrete subgroups.
  • RiemannianGeometry (merged): Curvature, Jacobi fields, submanifolds, and Bochner geometry. Develops Riemannian geometry from curvature onwards: Jacobi fields and index forms, comparison theorems and space forms, isometric immersions with the first and second variation of volume, focal points, and Bochner–Weitzenböck formulas. It also treats Schrödinger-type operators on compact manifolds.

It builds the group of C^{k,α} diffeomorphisms with its inverse function theorems and topologies, showing that the C^{k,α} topology does not make it a topological group while the topology of convergence in every lower Hölder exponent does. 33Domain of dependence and development of initial data for the compressible Euler equationscensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2442 §§1.2, 3.1–3.3 (Euler, acoustical form, D⁺_D(S)), §§8.1–8.4 (developments of type (i) data, maximal developments, null boundary); PR #3010 §2.2 (null-geodesic ruling of the characteristic boundary), §§3.2–3.3 (characteristic data and their developments); on PR #2232 §7.3, §12.6 and PR #2246
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • CompressibleEulerEquations (open PR #2442): Compressible Euler equations, symmetric hyperbolic systems and maximal developments. Develops the classical theory of the compressible Euler equations of a perfect fluid on ℝ × ℝⁿ: the barotropic and full systems, their symmetric hyperbolic form, sound cones and domains of dependence. Main results are local well-posedness in Sobolev spaces for data without vacuum, uniqueness on domains of dependence, and the theory of developments of initial data, including the existence of maximal developments and the fact that their regular boundary is null for the acoustical metric.
  • CompressibleEulerEquationsPartII (open PR #3010): Compressible Euler equations, part II: acoustical geometry and characteristic data. Builds the geometry of the acoustical metric of a solution of the compressible Euler equations, whose null cones are the sound cones: acoustical functions whose level sets are sound fronts, acoustical coordinates, and the characteristic boundary of a development, each point of which lies on a null geodesic issuing from the edge of the data. It then treats initial data prescribed partly on a characteristic hypersurface, with their developments, maximal developments, local existence near the edge and the Goursat problem, and shows that the plane-symmetric centered rarefaction wave of a polytropic gas fits this setting.
  • LorentzianGeometry (open PR #2232): Semi-Riemannian foundations and Lorentzian causality. A first part sets up semi-Riemannian foundations: scalar products of any signature, metrics of limited regularity and their Levi-Civita connection, geodesics, submanifold geometry and Jacobi fields. A second part develops Lorentzian geometry and causality: time orientations and spacetimes, causal curves, null hypersurfaces, global hyperbolicity, Cauchy surfaces and domains of dependence, and the singularity theorems of Hawking and Penrose.
  • SymmetricHyperbolicSystems (open PR #2246): Linear symmetric hyperbolic systems. Develops the classical theory of linear first-order systems on ℝ × ℝⁿ that are symmetric hyperbolic in the sense of Friedrichs, by Friedrichs' energy method and his finite difference scheme, without Fourier analysis or Sobolev spaces. For smooth coefficients with bounded derivatives and smooth compactly supported data, main results are existence of a C¹ solution of the Cauchy problem, uniqueness with a domain of dependence and explicit propagation speed, the L² energy estimate, and convergence of the scheme under the Courant–Friedrichs–Lewy condition.

Also cited in its census file: IncompressibleFluids, RelativisticQuantumFieldTheory.

34Neumann eigenvalues and eigenfunctions of the Laplacian on a bounded domaincensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizePDE §4.6 (with §§1.8, 4.5; §1.8 and §4.6 partly in code, §4.5 not started); abstract operator Tau Ceti IsCoercive.formOperator; Prop 34.3 boundary regularity unplanned

35Absolutely continuous spectrum of a self-adjoint operatorcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeUnboundedOperators UO-C03 (§3.3), after UO-C01, on SelfAdjointSpectralTheory SA-E01 (unbounded spectral PVM in code); σ_ac characterisations in PR #2424 §§1.2–1.5

37Caloric gauge and the manifold of caloric connectionscensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeYangMillsConnectionsAndCaloricGauge Layer 9, §§10.1–10.5
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • YangMillsConnectionsAndCaloricGauge (merged): Yang–Mills connections on Euclidean space and the caloric gauge. It then treats harmonic Yang–Mills connections on ℝ⁴ (energy gap and ground state), the Yang–Mills heat flow, and the caloric gauge of Oh and Tataru, ending with the theorem that the caloric connections form a C¹ submanifold of a Banach space.

Also cited in its census file: FourManifolds, InstantonFloerTheory.

38Fully nonlinear complex Hessian-type equations on a compact Hermitian manifoldcensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeComplexHessianEquations §§3.1–3.3 (§1.1 relative eigenvalues, §2.4 admissible pairs, Lewis's theorem, i∂∂̄u, the pointwise eigenvalue vectors of α + i∂∂̄φ and the eigenvalue function relEigenvalueFunction (38.1) in code, so 38.1–38.2 are in code); Hermitian and Kähler calculus ComplexKahlerGeometry K1/K2
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ComplexHessianEquations (merged): Complex Hessian equations. Treats fully nonlinear elliptic equations of complex Hessian type on compact complex manifolds, written through the eigenvalues of a real (1,1)-form relative to a Hermitian metric. It develops spectral functions, Gårding cones and admissible pairs, the comparison principle and uniqueness, and on compact Kähler manifolds Yau's theorem on the complex Monge–Ampère equation and the sharp L∞ estimates of Guo, Phong and Tong.
  • ComplexKahlerGeometry (merged): Complex manifolds, Dolbeault theory and Kähler geometry. Develops complex manifolds, Dolbeault theory and Kähler geometry: the Newlander–Nirenberg theorem, the Dolbeault–Grothendieck lemma, the local algebra of holomorphic germs, holomorphic and Hermitian bundles, and elliptic theory on compact Hermitian manifolds with Serre duality. 39Hard sphere dynamics on the toruscensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2448 §§3.1–3.3, 5.1–5.5, 6.4 (Alexander), 7.1–7.5, 8.1–8.2; almost-everywhere flows merged ClassicalErgodicTheory §3.5
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • HardSphereDynamicsAndKineticLimits (open PR #2448): Hard-sphere dynamics and kinetic limits. Builds the dynamics of finitely many hard spheres in Euclidean space or on a flat torus: elastic collisions, trajectories and, by Alexander's theorem, a measure-preserving flow defined off a null set. On this flow it develops the Liouville equation, marginals and correlation functions, equilibrium ensembles and the Boltzmann collision operator; Lanford's theorem on the Boltzmann–Grad limit is stated, and its law-of-large-numbers consequence is proved.
  • ClassicalErgodicTheory (merged): Classical ergodic theory, entropy, the variational principle and flows. Builds classical ergodic theory for measure-preserving maps, group actions and flows: factors and joinings, the mixing hierarchy and its spectral characterisations, Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy, topological entropy and pressure, and the variational principle. It defines equilibrium states and measures of maximal entropy, and proves Bowen's uniqueness theorem for expansive systems with specification and the Ruelle–Perron–Frobenius theorem.

Also cited in its census file: MathematicalBilliards.

40The Laplace–Beltrami operator on L2 of a complete Riemannian manifoldcensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2424 §§3.2–3.7 (Gaffney–Chernoff–Strichartz, spectral vocabulary), §3.10 ([1/4, ∞) ⊆ σ_ess at a cusp); closed case ComplexKahlerGeometry K5a, RiemannianGeometry R5c; UnboundedOperators UO-D06, UO-C02; congruence quotients CongruenceSpectralGap §§0.2, 0.6
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • SchrodingerOperatorsAndSpectralTypes (open PR #2424): Schrödinger operators and spectral types. Defines the absolutely continuous, singular continuous and pure point spectra of a self-adjoint operator and proves criteria for them, including the Kato–Rosenblum theorem on trace-class perturbations. It constructs the self-adjoint Laplace–Beltrami operator of a complete Riemannian manifold (the theorem of Gaffney, Chernoff and Strichartz) with its basic spectral properties, and the Schrödinger operators −Δ + V with bounded real potential on L²(ℝᵈ), with their self-adjointness, cores and essential spectrum.
  • ComplexKahlerGeometry (merged): Complex manifolds, Dolbeault theory and Kähler geometry. Develops complex manifolds, Dolbeault theory and Kähler geometry: the Newlander–Nirenberg theorem, the Dolbeault–Grothendieck lemma, the local algebra of holomorphic germs, holomorphic and Hermitian bundles, and elliptic theory on compact Hermitian manifolds with Serre duality. On compact Kähler manifolds it proves the Kähler identities, the Hodge decomposition, hard Lefschetz and the Hodge–Riemann relations.
  • OperatorTheory/UnboundedOperators (merged): Unbounded operators on Hilbert space. Develops closed, densely defined operators on Hilbert space: the unbounded Borel functional calculus, normal operators and joint spectral theory, the essential spectrum, and the decomposition of a self-adjoint operator into pure point, absolutely continuous and singular continuous parts. It also covers symmetric operators and their self-adjoint extensions, closed forms and the Friedrichs extension, analytic vectors, and perturbation theorems of Kato–Rellich type.
  • CongruenceSpectralGap (merged): The congruence spectral gap. Builds the Laplacian on the quotient of the upper half-plane by a congruence subgroup of SL₂(ℤ), with its spectral decomposition, and proves the lower bound 21/100 for the bottom of its positive spectrum. The proof goes through the Luo–Rudnick–Sarnak bound for unitary cuspidal representations of GLₘ over ℚ with spherical archimedean component.

Also cited in its census file: AutomorphicSpectralTheory, MaassForms.

41Quasiregular map between Riemannian manifoldscensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeQuasiregularMaps §§2.1–2.3; Sobolev class SobolevMapsBetweenManifolds §§4.2–4.3, 5.1, 6.1–6.3
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • QuasiregularMaps (merged): Quasiregular maps. Defines quasiregular maps between oriented Riemannian manifolds of the same dimension as continuous Sobolev maps satisfying a distortion inequality, and develops their outer, inner and maximal distortions. Main results are the comparison of these distortions, agreement with the classical definition on Euclidean domains, quasiregularity of suitable simplicial maps, and the first properties of quasiregularly elliptic manifolds, among them that every closed orientable flat manifold is quasiregularly elliptic.
  • SobolevMapsBetweenManifolds (merged): Sobolev maps between manifolds. Develops first-order Sobolev maps between manifolds: the local class of continuous maps whose chart representatives lie locally in the Sobolev space of exponent p, the weak differential, its norm and, for oriented Riemannian manifolds of equal dimension, the Jacobian. Main results are independence of charts and metrics, chain rules, pullback of differential forms commuting with the exterior derivative, smooth approximation, and a degree for Sobolev maps of exponent n between closed oriented manifolds of dimension n.

Also cited in its census file: SobolevDifferentialForms.

43SU(1,1) non-linear Fourier transform of an L2 function on the half-linecensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeDiracScatteringAndNonlinearFourierTransform §§5.1–5.5 (scattering limit, q⌃, convergence in Π-measure), on Layers 1–4 (§§1.1–1.5 and most of §1.6 in code; of Layer 2, §2.1, most of §2.2 and the maximal bound of §2.3 in code); Herglotz input AutomorphicSpectralTheory §0.5 (existence, uniqueness, the resolvent specialization and Checks in code)

48Topological class (instanton number) of a finite-energy connection on ℝ4census fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeYangMillsConnectionsAndCaloricGauge §§3.1–3.2, §§5.2–5.5, §6.4; closed-manifold analogue in FourManifolds Layer 5

50Lorentzian manifold, time orientation and spacetimecensus filePartly in codeTask file: reviewCode: ContMDiffNondegenerateMetric and its Levi-Civita connection (Completed/HopfRinow L1); full definition in PR #2232 §§6.1–6.2, 2.2–2.3, 2.7, 7.1, 8.1–8.2
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • Completed/HopfRinow (merged): Geodesics, the exponential map, and the Hopf–Rinow theorem. Develops the geodesic theory of Riemannian manifolds: covariant differentiation along curves, the geodesic equation and geodesic flow, the exponential map and its local inverse, normal neighbourhoods, the Gauss lemma and minimizing geodesics. The main result is the Hopf–Rinow theorem: on a connected Riemannian manifold, completeness of the exponential map at a point, compactness of closed bounded sets, metric completeness and geodesic completeness are equivalent, and each implies that any two points are joined by a minimizing geodesic.
  • LorentzianGeometry (open PR #2232): Semi-Riemannian foundations and Lorentzian causality. A first part sets up semi-Riemannian foundations: scalar products of any signature, metrics of limited regularity and their Levi-Civita connection, geodesics, submanifold geometry and Jacobi fields. A second part develops Lorentzian geometry and causality: time orientations and spacetimes, causal curves, null hypersurfaces, global hyperbolicity, Cauchy surfaces and domains of dependence, and the singularity theorems of Hawking and Penrose.

Also cited in its census file: PositiveMass, RelativisticQuantumFieldTheory, CompressibleEulerEquations.

53Stationary varifoldcensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeVarifolds §§1.1–1.4 (Grassmann bundle, varifolds; grassmannBundle, varifolds on it with weight, mass, support and restriction, and convergence VarifoldTendsto in code, so 53.1), §§2.2–2.3 (rectifiable, integral), §3.1 (push-forward), §§4.1, 4.3–4.4 (first variation, stationarity; Euclidean Euclidean.firstVariation, Euclidean.IsStationary and §4.2's Euclidean polarVector in code), §5.1, Layers 6–8 (monotonicity, tangent cones); inputs GeometricCurrents G6, GeometricMeasureTheory G2

  • GeometricCurrents (merged): Integral currents, geometric homology and minimizing hypersurfaces. Builds currents on Riemannian manifolds: mass, integer-rectifiable and integral currents, boundary, pushforward and slicing, and the homology of integral currents with its comparison to singular homology. It then proves deformation and compactness theorems, treats the Plateau problem and mass minimisation in a homology class, and proves interior regularity of area-minimising hypersurfaces in ambient dimension at most seven.
  • GeometricMeasureTheory (merged): Geometric measure theory: rectifiability, BV, and finite perimeter. Builds Euclidean geometric measure theory: rectifiable curves and arclength, rectifiable sets with the area and coarea formulas, functions of bounded variation and their variation measures, approximation and compactness, and sets of finite perimeter with their reduced boundaries. Main results are the structure theorem for sets of finite perimeter, the coarea formula for functions of bounded variation, traces and the Gauss–Green formula, giving the measure-theoretic basis used for the Euclidean isoperimetric inequality.

Also cited in its census file: #2491.

54Character variety of a surface group in a real reductive groupcensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2430 §§4.3, 5.2, 6.1, 6.7, 7.1; real forms and Cartan involutions in RealSemisimpleLieGroups §2.5; inputs LieGroups L7, L9
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • SurfaceGroupCharacterVarieties (open PR #2430): Surface group character varieties. Constructs the outer action of the mapping class group of a punctured surface on its fundamental group, the representation scheme of a finitely generated group, and the character variety of such a group in a real reductive group, the space of reductive representations modulo conjugation. Main results are that the character variety is Hausdorff, locally compact and second countable, that for complex groups it is homeomorphic to the complex points of the affine quotient, and that the mapping class group acts on it by homeomorphisms.
  • RealSemisimpleLieGroups (merged): Real semisimple Lie groups, parabolic subgroups and Riemannian symmetric spaces. Builds the structure theory of real semisimple and reductive Lie groups on top of the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions: Cartan involutions, restricted roots, the real rank, the restricted Weyl group, minimal and general parabolic subgroups with their Langlands decompositions, and the Bruhat decomposition. It then develops the Riemannian symmetric space G/K: its invariant metric, curvature and geodesics, flats and rank, the conjugacy of maximal compact subgroups, and quotients by discrete subgroups.
  • RepresentationTheory/LieGroups (merged): Lie groups and the Lie algebra correspondence. Builds the correspondence between Lie groups and Lie algebras: the exponential map, the adjoint representation, the closed-subgroup theorem, the Lie functor and Lie's third theorem. It continues with the structure of compact and reductive groups: maximal tori and the Weyl integration formula, complexification and real forms, the Borel–Weil theorem, and the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions with restricted roots.

Also cited in its census file: ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, LieGroupsPartII, MordellLawrenceVenkatesh, TeichmullerGeometry, MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations, NonabelianLevelStructures.

58MCG-finite representation of a surface groupcensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2266 §2.1 (MCG-finite, with the Mod(g, n) orbit form and base-point independence), §0.2 (PMod, its action on π₁, Dehn–Nielsen–Baer); Mod(S) from merged MordellLawrenceVenkatesh §5.9; Mod_{g,n} action on conjugacy classes also in PR #2430 §3.5
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • MappingClassGroupsAndCanonicalRepresentations (open PR #2266): Mapping class groups and canonical representations of surface groups. Builds the theory behind the theorem of Landesman and Litt: a representation of the fundamental group of a punctured surface of genus g, of rank r with r² < g + 1, whose conjugacy class has finite orbit under the mapping class group has finite image. It develops pure mapping class groups acting on surface groups, representation schemes, versal families of pointed curves, and the Hodge theory of unitary local systems on families of punctured curves.
  • MordellLawrenceVenkatesh (merged): The Mordell conjecture after Lawrence and Venkatesh. Proves Faltings's theorem, that a curve of genus at least two over a number field has finitely many rational points, by the method of Lawrence and Venkatesh, and the finiteness of solutions of the S-unit equation by the same method. Along the way it builds p-adic period maps for families of abelian varieties, the Kodaira–Parshin family, and the surface topology and mapping class groups used to show that its monodromy is large.
  • SurfaceGroupCharacterVarieties (open PR #2430): Surface group character varieties. Constructs the outer action of the mapping class group of a punctured surface on its fundamental group, the representation scheme of a finitely generated group, and the character variety of such a group in a real reductive group, the space of reductive representations modulo conjugation. Main results are that the character variety is Hausdorff, locally compact and second countable, that for complex groups it is homeomorphic to the complex points of the affine quotient, and that the mapping class group acts on it by homeomorphisms.

Also cited in its census file: FreeGroupsAndLimitGroups, GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfaces, TeichmullerGeometry.

59Mapping class group of a punctured surfacecensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: smooth TauCeti.MappingClassGroup (compact, ∂ fixed); MordellLawrenceVenkatesh §5.1 (punctured surfaces), §5.9 (Mod(S)); TeichmullerGeometry §0.0h (MappingClassGroupFull, in code); PR #2430 §§2.2–2.3 (extension over punctures, Sym(P)); PR #2266 §0.2 (PMod)
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • MordellLawrenceVenkatesh (merged): The Mordell conjecture after Lawrence and Venkatesh. Proves Faltings's theorem, that a curve of genus at least two over a number field has finitely many rational points, by the method of Lawrence and Venkatesh, and the finiteness of solutions of the S-unit equation by the same method. Along the way it builds p-adic period maps for families of abelian varieties, the Kodaira–Parshin family, and the surface topology and mapping class groups used to show that its monodromy is large.
  • TeichmullerGeometry (merged): Teichmüller geometry of closed surfaces. 67Local components of an automorphic representation (restricted tensor product)census fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeAutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §§3.4–3.5; carrier ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces ALS.F.6; prerequisites in SmoothRepresentationsOfLocalGroups, AdelicAlgebraicGroups
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups (merged): Automorphic forms on reductive groups. Builds the analytic and representation-theoretic setting for automorphic forms on a connected reductive group over a number field: smooth, Schwartz and moderate-growth functions on the adelic group, Harish-Chandra modules of the real group with infinitesimal characters, discrete series and archimedean Langlands parameters, automorphic representations with their local components, and constant terms and cuspidality. It ends with cohomological and coherent representations and with explicit dictionaries for GL₁, GL₂ and GSp₄, including the passage from classical modular forms to adelic ones.
  • ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces (merged): Arithmetic locally symmetric spaces and their cohomology. Builds the locally symmetric spaces attached to a connected reductive group over a number field and a compact open level, and their cohomology with integral coefficients. It covers the decomposition into arithmetic quotients of the symmetric space, neat levels, local systems, the Borel–Serre compactification, Hecke actions, duality, and the comparison with automorphic forms.
  • SmoothRepresentationsOfLocalGroups (merged): Smooth representations of local groups. Builds the smooth representation theory of locally profinite groups over a general coefficient ring, and of reductive p-adic groups in particular: Hecke algebras, parabolic induction and Jacquet functors, admissibility, second adjointness, the Bernstein decomposition and centre, and the Satake isomorphism. These are the local representations and Hecke algebras that the global automorphic theory uses.
  • AdelicAlgebraicGroups (merged): Adelic algebraic groups and arithmetic quotients. Develops the adelic points of affine algebraic groups over number fields: their topology, Haar and Tamagawa measures, arithmetic quotients, reduction theory, strong approximation and changes of level. Main results include finiteness of class numbers, finite volume and compactness criteria for the adelic quotient, and the theorem that S-arithmetic subgroups of connected reductive groups without rational characters are lattices; arithmetic lattices in real Lie groups are defined here.

Also cited in its census file: AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors, AutomorphicSpectralTheory, GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer, AutomorphicSpectralTheoryPartII, LevelOneAutomorphicFormsForClassicalGroups.

68Arithmetic lattice in a semisimple Lie groupcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeAdelicAlgebraicGroups AA.3.4; Mathlib Subgroup.IsArithmetic (GL₂(ℝ)); PU(1,n) case in BallQuotientsAndComplexHyperbolicLattices §11.1, PSL₂(ℝ) case in ArithmeticFuchsianGroups §1.1
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AdelicAlgebraicGroups (merged): Adelic algebraic groups and arithmetic quotients. Develops the adelic points of affine algebraic groups over number fields: their topology, Haar and Tamagawa measures, arithmetic quotients, reduction theory, strong approximation and changes of level. Main results include finiteness of class numbers, finite volume and compactness criteria for the adelic quotient, and the theorem that S-arithmetic subgroups of connected reductive groups without rational characters are lattices; arithmetic lattices in real Lie groups are defined here.
  • BallQuotientsAndComplexHyperbolicLattices (merged): Ball quotients and complex hyperbolic lattices. Studies the complex ball as the symmetric space of PU(1,n) and the lattices in this group, arithmetic or not: cusps, finite generation, Selberg's lemma, and the quotient of the ball as a normal complex space. It constructs the toroidal and minimal compactifications and proves that the minimal compactification is a normal projective variety, so that every ball quotient is quasi-projective, and that the quotient by a torsion-free lattice has a unique algebraic structure; in the arithmetic case it compares with the Baily–Borel compactification. It ends with totally geodesic and special subvarieties.
  • ArithmeticFuchsianGroups (merged): Arithmetic Fuchsian groups, commensurators and isospectral covers. Develops the arithmetic theory of lattices in PSL₂(ℝ) coming from orders in quaternion algebras over totally real fields split at exactly one real place. Main results are discreteness, finite covolume and the covolume formula for maximal orders, the finiteness theorems of Borel for bounded covolume and of Takeuchi for a given signature, Margulis' criterion that a lattice is arithmetic exactly when its commensurator is dense, and nonisometric isospectral degree-eight covers of every closed hyperbolic surface of genus at least three with nonarithmetic maximal uniformizing group.

Also cited in its census file: ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, KleinianGroups, RealSemisimpleLieGroups, TeichmullerGeometry.

69Galois representation attached to a cuspidal Hecke eigenformcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeAlgebraicModularFormsAndSerreWeights §6.2 (higherWeight_attachment), on §6.1 and ModularCurvesPartII §§14.1–14.4a, 14.11; carrier in ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations Layer 1; DFG comparison in AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations §§1.5, 1.7

  • ModularCurvesPartII (merged): Modular curves, Part II — uniformization, integral boundary and Hecke cohomology.   • AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations (merged): Galois representations attached to modular and Hilbert modular forms. Realises the Galois representations of cuspidal eigenforms of weight at least two in parabolic cohomology and compares them with the motivic construction, constructs those of Hilbert modular forms, and places the Artin representations of weight-one forms in the same normalisation. Results include local compatibility at every place, compatible systems, ordinary and crystalline properties, large-image theorems, and maps from deformation rings to integral Hecke algebras.

70Abelian scheme with complex multiplication by OL of CM type Σcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §1.2 (eigenspaces, lifted CM types, CM by O_L of type Σ, Lie and Hodge comparison); AutomorphicPadicLFunctionsPartII §§0.1–0.2; CM types ShimuraVarieties §5.1, CM-field case PELModuli M1.3; abelian schemes in AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli §§A0.1, A1.3, A4.3 (AbelianScheme in code)
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity (merged): Complex multiplication and explicit reciprocity. Develops complex multiplication starting from CM types and reflex norms: abelian schemes with complex multiplication and their twists by ideals, polarised CM lattices, the reciprocity law on torsion points, class polynomials and the class fields they generate, and the Hecke characters of CM abelian varieties. It also treats reduction of CM abelian varieties and the isogeny graphs that arise.
  • AutomorphicPadicLFunctionsPartII (merged): Automorphic p-adic L-functions, part II — critical Hecke values. Studies the critical values of L-functions of algebraic Hecke characters of a totally imaginary field containing a CM field, through Eisenstein–Kronecker classes on abelian schemes with complex multiplication. Its results are the integrality and algebraicity of these values after division by CM periods, and their p-adic interpolation by measures in the ordinary case.
  • ShimuraVarieties (merged): Complex Shimura varieties and canonical models. Constructs complex Shimura varieties as towers of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains, their Baily–Borel compactifications, and canonical models over reflex fields: first the Siegel case by complex multiplication, then Hodge and abelian type, then general Shimura data. It also treats Mumford–Tate groups and the Galois representations of abelian varieties with values in them.
  • PELModuli (merged): Siegel and PEL moduli problems. Builds the moduli problems of abelian schemes with a polarization, an action of an order and a level structure, which include the Siegel, Hilbert and unitary cases. It starts from the linear algebra of lattices with an order action and their similitude groups, proves that the moduli problem is a smooth algebraic stack over the appropriate base, and identifies its complex points with a union of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains.
  • AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli (merged): Abelian schemes and arithmetic moduli (the Jacobian challenge, part II). Develops abelian schemes over an arbitrary base: rigidity and the theorem of the cube in families, the dual abelian scheme, polarizations, isogenies and Weil pairings, Tate modules with the de Rham and crystalline realizations, and the deformation theorems of Serre–Tate and Grothendieck–Messing. Over the complex numbers it gives the lattice uniformization, Riemann forms, the Appell–Humbert theorem and the equivalence between polarized abelian varieties and polarized Hodge structures of weight one. Over a field it proves the basic arithmetic of homomorphisms, including finiteness of Hom and positivity of the Rosati involution.

Also cited in its census file: AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliPartII.

71Abelian variety Af attached to a weight-2 newformcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeModularCurvesPartII §16.5 (J₀(N)/B_f⁰, N ≥ 5); J₁(N) variant §16.2 = CyclotomicClassGroupsAndStickelberger §4.3; consumers EllipticCurveModularity (A_F⁰), PR #1036

  • EllipticCurveModularityPartIIGL2TypeAbelianVarieties (open PR #1036): Abelian varieties of GL₂-type and modularity of ℚ-curves. Studies abelian varieties of GL₂-type over ℚ, their endomorphism algebras and their λ-adic representations. It proves that each is a quotient of a modular Jacobian J₁(N), with the expected conductor and level, and deduces the modularity of ℚ-curves.

Builds the analytic and representation-theoretic setting for automorphic forms on a connected reductive group over a number field: smooth, Schwartz and moderate-growth functions on the adelic group, Harish-Chandra modules of the real group with infinitesimal characters, discrete series and archimedean Langlands parameters, automorphic representations with their local components, and constant terms and cuspidality. It ends with cohomological and coherent representations and with explicit dictionaries for GL₁, GL₂ and GSp₄, including the passage from classical modular forms to adelic ones.
  • AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations (merged): Galois representations attached to modular and Hilbert modular forms. Realises the Galois representations of cuspidal eigenforms of weight at least two in parabolic cohomology and compares them with the motivic construction, constructs those of Hilbert modular forms, and places the Artin representations of weight-one forms in the same normalisation. Results include local compatibility at every place, compatible systems, ordinary and crystalline properties, large-image theorems, and maps from deformation rings to integral Hecke algebras.
  • ModularityAndLanglandsExtensionsPartII (open PR #2449): Modularity and Langlands extensions, part II — the proof of symmetric power functoriality. Carries out the proof of symmetric power functoriality for holomorphic modular forms: analytic continuation along eigenvarieties, level raising on unitary groups, automorphy lifting for symmetric powers, and functoriality for tensor products. The final results are the existence of every symmetric power lift of a non-CM newform of weight at least 2, and the holomorphy of the symmetric power L-functions of such newforms away from the level and of level-one eigenforms.

Also cited in its census file: AdelicAlgebraicGroups, ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpacesPartII, AutomorphicSpectralTheory, BianchiModularForms, EllipticCurveModularityImaginaryQuadraticPartII, ModularityAndLanglandsExtensions, PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems, SmoothRepresentationsOfLocalGroups.

74Complex CM periods of a CM abelian schemecensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeAutomorphicPadicLFunctionsPartII §§0.2, 5.1; period pairing in ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §1.2; inputs AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli §§A4.3, A5.1
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AutomorphicPadicLFunctionsPartII (merged): Automorphic p-adic L-functions, part II — critical Hecke values. Studies the critical values of L-functions of algebraic Hecke characters of a totally imaginary field containing a CM field, through Eisenstein–Kronecker classes on abelian schemes with complex multiplication. Its results are the integrality and algebraicity of these values after division by CM periods, and their p-adic interpolation by measures in the ordinary case.
  • ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity (merged): Complex multiplication and explicit reciprocity. Develops complex multiplication starting from CM types and reflex norms: abelian schemes with complex multiplication and their twists by ideals, polarised CM lattices, the reciprocity law on torsion points, class polynomials and the class fields they generate, and the Hecke characters of CM abelian varieties. It also treats reduction of CM abelian varieties and the isogeny graphs that arise.
  • AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli (merged): Abelian schemes and arithmetic moduli (the Jacobian challenge, part II). Develops abelian schemes over an arbitrary base: rigidity and the theorem of the cube in families, the dual abelian scheme, polarizations, isogenies and Weil pairings, Tate modules with the de Rham and crystalline realizations, and the deformation theorems of Serre–Tate and Grothendieck–Messing. Over the complex numbers it gives the lattice uniformization, Riemann forms, the Appell–Humbert theorem and the equivalence between polarized abelian varieties and polarized Hodge structures of weight one. Over a field it proves the basic arithmetic of homomorphisms, including finiteness of Hom and positivity of the Rosati involution.

Also cited in its census file: AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliPartII, ComplexComparisonPartII, ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocityPartII, ShimuraVarieties.
