77Emerton's completed cohomology of modular curvescensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeCompletedCohomologyPartII §2.1, §0.5, Layer 1, §§8.4, 8.7; modular-tower normalizations in CompletedCohomologyAndLocalGlobalCompatibility §§1.1–1.2; finite-level X_K in ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces ALS.0.6, ALS.1; level-H curves in ModularCurvesPartII §3.12; compactified variant has no target
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • CompletedCohomologyPartII (merged): Completed cohomology, homology and arithmetic towers — Part II. Defines completed cohomology and completed homology of a tower of arithmetic locally symmetric spaces: with torsion coefficients one takes the colimit over the levels at p and then the limit over the coefficients. It proves finiteness and admissibility over the Iwasawa algebra, descent to finite level and duality, and compares the result with étale cohomology for the modular, Siegel, unitary and Hilbert towers, with their Galois and Hecke actions.
  • CompletedCohomologyAndLocalGlobalCompatibility (merged): Completed cohomology and p-adic local–global compatibility over ℚ. Specialises completed cohomology to modular curves and quaternionic Shimura curves over ℚ and studies it as a representation of GL₂(ℚₚ), of the Galois group and of the Hecke algebra. Its main results are Emerton's local–global compatibility with the p-adic local Langlands correspondence, in Banach, mod p and homological forms, with applications to patched modules in modularity lifting.
  • ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces (merged): Arithmetic locally symmetric spaces and their cohomology. Builds the locally symmetric spaces attached to a connected reductive group over a number field and a compact open level, and their cohomology with integral coefficients. It covers the decomposition into arithmetic quotients of the symmetric space, neat levels, local systems, the Borel–Serre compactification, Hecke actions, duality, and the comparison with automorphic forms.
  • ModularCurvesPartII (merged): Modular curves, Part II — uniformization, integral boundary and Hecke cohomology. Compares arithmetic modular curves with analytic quotients of the upper half-plane, builds their integral compactifications and bad fibres, and constructs the Hecke action on their Jacobians and cohomology. Results include compact uniformisation with the fields of definition of the cusps, Ihara's theorems, and, for the abelian varieties attached to newforms of weight 2, the corresponding quotients of J₀(N), oldform factors and Abel–Jacobi maps.

Also cited in its census file: AdelicAlgebraicGroups, HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves, LocallyAnalyticDistributions, HigherHidaAndColemanTheory.

79Minimal parabolic subgroups and the Langlands decomposition (real rank one)census fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeAutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §2.3a; finite-centre groups RealSemisimpleLieGroups §3.1; all reductive Cartan data LieGroupsPartII Layer 3; inputs LieGroups L9
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups (merged): Automorphic forms on reductive groups. Builds the analytic and representation-theoretic setting for automorphic forms on a connected reductive group over a number field: smooth, Schwartz and moderate-growth functions on the adelic group, Harish-Chandra modules of the real group with infinitesimal characters, discrete series and archimedean Langlands parameters, automorphic representations with their local components, and constant terms and cuspidality.   • RepresentationTheory/LieGroups (merged): Lie groups and the Lie algebra correspondence. Builds the correspondence between Lie groups and Lie algebras: the exponential map, the adjoint representation, the closed-subgroup theorem, the Lie functor and Lie's third theorem. It continues with the structure of compact and reductive groups: maximal tori and the Weyl integration formula, complexification and real forms, the Borel–Weil theorem, and the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions with restricted roots.

80The Néron–Tate (canonical) height on an abelian varietycensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeHeightsRationalPointsAndObstructions §§0.18–0.21 (code has the elliptic-curve case and §0.18's Tate limit tateLimit); Weil height class in ArakelovGeometryAndAbelianHeights §1.13; ĥ = ½ĥ_Θ in ArakelovGeometryAndAbelianHeightsPartII §8.4

82The quasi-split unitary group of a self-dual hermitian latticecensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeIgusaVarietiesAndTorsionConcentration §§0.3–0.4; PELModuli M0.4 SymplecticOLattice.dual, M0.7 PELDatum.isometryGroup, M5.5; unitary integral models in HermitianFormsAndLattices §4.6; code ConstantForm.groupScheme
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • IgusaVarietiesAndTorsionConcentration (merged): Igusa varieties, compactified period fibres and torsion concentration. Proves Koshikawa's vanishing theorem for unitary Shimura varieties: at a prime split completely in the CM field, with hyperspecial level, localization at a generic maximal ideal of the spherical Hecke algebra confines mod-ℓ cohomology to one side of the middle degree, with integral and boundary consequences (Caraiani–Newton in the form needed when F⁺ = ℚ). The route is Mantovan's filtration through Igusa varieties and local Shimura varieties, Koshikawa's semiperversity theorem for the local factor, and partial toroidal and minimal compactifications of central leaves and Igusa varieties identified with the fibres of the Hodge–Tate period map. Layer 0 sets up the quasi-split unitary similitude datum over a CM field with a self-dual lattice, its Hecke algebras, p-divisible groups with extra structure, Rapoport–Zink spaces and central leaves.
  • PELModuli (merged): Siegel and PEL moduli problems. Builds the moduli problems of abelian schemes with a polarization, an action of an order and a level structure, which include the Siegel, Hilbert and unitary cases. It starts from the linear algebra of lattices with an order action and their similitude groups, proves that the moduli problem is a smooth algebraic stack over the appropriate base, and identifies its complex points with a union of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains.
  • RiemannHypothesisForDirichletLFunctions/HermitianFormsAndLattices (merged): Hermitian forms and lattices. Develops the arithmetic of Hermitian and skew-Hermitian forms over quadratic étale algebras and of their lattices: unitary group schemes and their reductive structure, Witt theory and the local and global classification of Hermitian spaces, and Hermitian lattices over rings of integers with their duals, integral models, genera, class sets and masses. Its main results are the Tamagawa number two of unitary groups, the mass formula, Siegel's main theorem and Hermitian theta series of definite lattices.

Also cited in its census file: ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactorsPartII, EndoscopicTransferAndUnitaryTraceComparison, GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic, GrossZagierAndArithmeticHeightsPartII, ModularityAndLanglandsExtensionsPartII, PELModuliPartII.

84Selmer sections and the localisation mapcensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #1739 §§0.1–0.3; Sect(X/k) and κ_X in AnabelianGeometryAndNonabelianChabauty §7.1; exact sequence and point sections in EtaleCohomologyFoundations §F.0.A2; Abel–Jacobi immersion in JacobianChallenge Layer F
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • GaloisSectionsPadicPeriodMaps (open PR #1739): Galois sections and p-adic period mappings. Studies sections of the fundamental exact sequence of a variety over a number field by means of p-adic period maps. It defines Selmer sections and their localisation maps and proves, for a smooth projective curve of genus at least two over a number field with no CM subfield, that only finitely many local points at each finite place arise from Selmer sections.

Builds étale cohomology of schemes with finite, adic and rational coefficients: Nagata compactification, constructible sheaves, proper and smooth base change, cohomological dimension, Artin vanishing and finiteness, purity, duality and perverse sheaves. Its main results are the six operations on bounded constructible complexes with their base change, Künneth and duality theorems, and the exact sequence of arithmetic fundamental groups.

For a smooth proper geometrically connected curve over a field, builds the Jacobian as an abelian variety representing the degree-zero Picard functor, without assuming a rational point, and, for each rational point, the Abel–Jacobi map with its universal property. Along the way it develops Riemann–Roch and Serre duality on curves, relative Picard schemes, and abelian varieties with their duals, polarizations and theta divisor.

Also cited in its census file: InverseGaloisAndArithmeticFundamentalGroups, LocalAnabelianGeometry, LocalAnabelianGeometryPartII.

What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactorsPartII (merged): Automorphic L-functions and local factors, part II. Develops the doubling method for standard L-functions: classical doubling for unitary groups, with its archimedean sections, and generalised doubling for split symplectic, special orthogonal and general spin groups. Its main results are local functional equations, entireness of the global L-functions after highly ramified twists, and a weak transfer of automorphic representations of these groups to general linear groups.
  • AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors (merged): Automorphic L-functions and local factors. Develops the analytic theory of L-functions of characters and of automorphic representations of GLₙ: local and adelic Fourier analysis, the Godement–Jacquet and Rankin–Selberg integrals, and the local factors they define. It ends by comparing Satake and Weil–Deligne determinants and by treating rational period structures.
  • ModularityAndLanglandsExtensions (merged): Modularity and Langlands extensions. Extends modularity results to Artin representations, potential automorphy, symmetric power functoriality and the Sato–Tate conjecture, and treats packets for classical groups. It sets up the comparisons of normalizations these results need and states the remaining Langlands conjectures precisely.

Also cited in its census file: AdelicAlgebraicGroups, ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups, EndoscopicTransferAndUnitaryTraceComparison, MetaplecticAutomorphicForms, SmoothRepresentationsOfLocalGroups, ModularityAndLanglandsExtensionsPartII.

89ℓ-adic Galois representation with values in the Mumford–Tate groupcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeShimuraVarieties §7.0i.1, on §§7.0i, P.3a (code: Mumford–Tate Hopf ideal mumfordTateHopfIdeal); Conj_G in ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations §1.11 (code: conjugationQuotient)
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ShimuraVarieties (merged): Complex Shimura varieties and canonical models. Constructs complex Shimura varieties as towers of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains, their Baily–Borel compactifications, and canonical models over reflex fields: first the Siegel case by complex multiplication, then Hodge and abelian type, then general Shimura data. It also treats Mumford–Tate groups and the Galois representations of abelian varieties with values in them.
  • ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations (merged): Arithmetic Galois representations and conductors. Develops continuous representations of absolute Galois groups with their integral models and reductions, their restrictions to decomposition groups with Frobenius polynomials and Weil–Deligne representations, Artin and Swan conductors, and the Tate modules of elliptic curves and abelian varieties. It also provides symmetric and other powers of representations and the conditions on residual images used in automorphy lifting.

Also cited in its census file: AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli, ComplexComparisonPartII, IntegralHeckeAndGaloisDeterminants, NeronModelsAndSemistableAbelianVarieties, ReductiveGroupsPartIII, SatoTateGroups, ShimuraData, ShimuraVarietiesPartII.

90The π-nearly isotypic cuspidal subspacecensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #1761 §4.2a; inputs in AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §§3.4–3.5, 4.2
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • AutomorphicSpectralTheoryPartII (open PR #1761): Automorphic spectral theory and trace distributions, part II. Develops multipliers of the Schwartz algebra of a reductive group over a number field: entire functions of controlled growth give multipliers on the real group, which are combined with spherical Hecke operators at finite places. The main results use such multipliers to isolate prescribed parts of the cuspidal spectrum, with the supporting analysis of weighted function spaces on automorphic quotients.
  • AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups (merged): Automorphic forms on reductive groups. Builds the analytic and representation-theoretic setting for automorphic forms on a connected reductive group over a number field: smooth, Schwartz and moderate-growth functions on the adelic group, Harish-Chandra modules of the real group with infinitesimal characters, discrete series and archimedean Langlands parameters, automorphic representations with their local components, and constant terms and cuspidality. It ends with cohomological and coherent representations and with explicit dictionaries for GL₁, GL₂ and GSp₄, including the passage from classical modular forms to adelic ones.

Also cited in its census file: AdelicAlgebraicGroups, ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces, AutomorphicSpectralTheory, SmoothRepresentationsOfLocalGroups.

91Anosov flowcensus fileRoadmap PRTask file: reviewPR #2441 §7.2 (IsFlowHyperbolicSet, Anosov flows), §§7.3–7.6, Layer 8; code: flowOf, continuous distributions, Riemannian metrics; exponential mixing in merged ClassicalErgodicTheory §3.6
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • HyperbolicDynamicsAndThermodynamicFormalism (open PR #2441): Hyperbolic dynamics and thermodynamic formalism. Builds the theory of uniformly hyperbolic diffeomorphisms and flows on manifolds: hyperbolic sets and Anosov systems, stable and unstable manifolds, local product structure, expansivity and specification. It joins this to the thermodynamic formalism, proving that a Hölder potential on a basic set has exactly one equilibrium state (the theorems of Bowen and of Bowen and Ruelle), the uniqueness of the measure of maximal entropy, and the description of the SRB measure of a hyperbolic attractor of a flow.
  • ClassicalErgodicTheory (merged): Classical ergodic theory, entropy, the variational principle and flows. Builds classical ergodic theory for measure-preserving maps, group actions and flows: factors and joinings, the mixing hierarchy and its spectral characterisations, Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy, topological entropy and pressure, and the variational principle. It defines equilibrium states and measures of maximal entropy, and proves Bowen's uniqueness theorem for expansive systems with specification and the Ruelle–Perron–Frobenius theorem.

Also cited in its census file: #1797.

92Billiard ball map of a convex billiard tablecensus fileIn Tau CetiTask file: statementsCode: StrictConvexBilliardTable.nextCollision (lifted map), billiardMap, billiardHomeomorph (homeomorphism), closedBilliardHomeomorph (closed cylinder), generatingFunction (twist), measurePreserving_billiardMap (area), IsInvariantGraph.rotationNumber (92.5 in graph form, as the source's third Remark allows), IsRotationalInvariantCurve (curves, §5.1); their rotation numbers in MathematicalBilliards Layer 5, all of Def 92 for C^k tables in Layers 1–5; C^∞ case in CotangentLagrangian C3.1
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • MathematicalBilliards (merged): Mathematical billiards. Builds the two classical planar billiard systems. For a strictly convex table it constructs the billiard ball map on the phase cylinder, shows that it is a twist map preserving the measure sin δ dδ ds, and proves Birkhoff's theorems on rotational invariant curves and on periodic orbits. For a polygonal table it constructs the phase space, the billiard flow and the translation surface obtained by unfolding a rational polygon, and for general measure-preserving flows it develops weak mixing.
  • SymplecticGeometry/CotangentLagrangian (merged): Cotangent bundles and Lagrangian geometry. Develops the symplectic geometry of cotangent bundles and Lagrangian submanifolds: the cotangent bundle as a phase space, embedded Lagrangians, generating functions and the canonical transformations they define, and neighbourhood theorems for symplectic germs along a submanifold. Convex planar billiards appear as an example of a map given by a generating function.

94Borel complete class of countable structurescensus filePartly in codeTask file: formalizeCode: RelationalConfiguration.BorelComplete for relational languages, with transfer, bireducibility and 94.4(3)–(4) (CountableStructureSpacesPartII §§1.1–1.2), on Setoid.BorelReducible, RelationalConfiguration, classIso; function-symbol graph-code transfer FunctionalConfiguration.classIso in code; Borel model classes (§1.3, open Tau Ceti PR https://git.taucetiproject.org/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/pulls/4702), Layers 2–6; coding space in CountableStructureSpaces
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • CountableStructureSpacesPartII (merged): Countable structure spaces, Part II: Borel reducibility of isomorphism relations and Borel completeness. Compares the isomorphism relations of classes of countable structures by Borel reducibility and defines Borel completeness, also for languages with function symbols. It proves the Friedman–Stanley theorem that countable graphs, trees and linear orders are Borel complete, the Becker–Kechris universality theorem for actions of the infinite symmetric group, and that a Borel complete isomorphism relation is not Borel.
  • CountableStructureSpaces (merged): Spaces of countable structures and the López–Escobar theorem. Builds the Polish space of structures of a countable relational language with universe ℕ, the action of the infinite symmetric group by relabelling, and the basic invariant descriptive set theory of this space. Its main result is the López–Escobar theorem: a class of countable structures is Borel and isomorphism-invariant exactly when it is the class of models of a sentence of the infinitary logic Lω₁ω. It also proves that every isomorphism class is Borel and that the class of well-orders is not.

Also cited in its census file: BorelEquivalenceRelations.

95Dynamical degree of a dominant rational self-mapcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeDynamicalDegreesOfRationalMaps §1.2 (graph, in code: Scheme.RationalMap.graph), §4.1 (mixedDegree), §4.4, Layer 5 (dynamicalDegree); N¹ and nef classes in AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometryPartII §§1.1, 1.5, 1.7 (code: numericallyTrivialClasses, Num(X/k), Num(X/S), RealNumericalClasses, LineBundleClass.IsNef); Mathlib rational maps
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • DynamicalDegreesOfRationalMaps (merged): Dynamical degrees of rational maps. Builds the degree theory of dominant rational maps between projective varieties over an arbitrary field: the graph of a rational map, intersection numbers, and the positivity inequalities of Siu and of Khovanskii and Teissier. Its main results are the existence of the dynamical degrees as limits, their independence of the polarisation, their log-concavity and their invariance under birational conjugacy; the algebraic entropy is defined from them.
  • AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometryPartII (merged): Algebraic moduli for arithmetic geometry, part II: numerical Picard groups and contraction descent. Develops numerical classes of invertible sheaves and Cartier divisors on proper schemes, over a field and over Noetherian bases, and uses them to descend contractions. It constructs relative numerical groups, cones of curves, nefness and Kleiman's criterion, and the Picard scheme of a proper scheme with its local system of numerical classes. Main results are a positive-power criterion for constancy of numerical classes, descent of geometric contractions and of fibre graphs of surfaces, comparisons over discrete valuation rings, and independence of the Alexeev–Hacon–Kawamata Picard rank from the resolution.

Also cited in its census file: ArithmeticDynamics, MotivesAndAlgebraicCycles, AbundanceStatement.

96Equilibrium state (measure) of a Hölder potentialcensus fileMerged roadmapTask file: formalizeClassicalErgodicTheory §§5.4–5.5 (pressure), §§7.1, 7.3, §8.1 IsEquilibriumState, IsFlowEquilibriumState, §§8.4–8.6, KS entropy §4.0; Bowen–Ruelle for Anosov flows in PR #2441 §10.3; Mathlib Dynamics.coverEntropy; KS entropy also in GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic §4.2 and ArithmeticDynamics §§2.6–2.7
What the cited roadmaps contain
  • ClassicalErgodicTheory (merged): Classical ergodic theory, entropy, the variational principle and flows. Builds classical ergodic theory for measure-preserving maps, group actions and flows: factors and joinings, the mixing hierarchy and its spectral characterisations, Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy, topological entropy and pressure, and the variational principle. It includes the homogeneous dynamics behind Margulis's theorem on values of indefinite quadratic forms and Duke's equidistribution theorem, with the entropy of measure-preserving maps, as well as LLL-reduced bases and the hermitian K-theory of exact categories.

Develops the arithmetic of iterated maps, especially rational maps of the projective line: preperiodic points, canonical heights, local escape functions and invariant measures, dynatomic curves, and Galois actions on trees of iterated preimages. Main results include equidistribution of small points and of periodic points, the facts that z² + c over ℚ has at most one rational 3-cycle and no rational 4- or 5-cycles, Stoll's surjective arboreal representations, Ritt's classification of polynomials with equal iterates, uniform common torsion images for Legendre curves, and dynamical Mordell–Lang for étale maps.

Also cited in its census file: ProbabilisticAndMetricNumberTheoryPartII.

By roadmap

Every roadmap and roadmap PR that the census cites, with a short description of what it contains and the definitions that cite it. The definitions listed first are those whose row in the table cites the roadmap, with the sections the row gives. Those listed as cited in the evidence have the roadmap in their census file only: there it can be a prerequisite, a neighbouring notion, or one the census found not to cover the definition. The area and status filters keep the roadmaps cited by a definition of that area and status.

AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliAbelian schemes and arithmetic moduli (the Jacobian challenge, part II)MergedDevelops abelian schemes over an arbitrary base: rigidity and the theorem of the cube in families, the dual abelian scheme, polarizations, isogenies and Weil pairings, Tate modules with the de Rham and crystalline realizations, and the deformation theorems of Serre–Tate and Grothendieck–Messing. Over the complex numbers it gives the lattice uniformization, Riemann forms, the Appell–Humbert theorem and the equivalence between polarized abelian varieties and polarized Hodge structures of weight one. Over a field it proves the basic arithmetic of homomorphisms, including finiteness of Hom and positivity of the Rosati involution.

ArcTopologyAndDescentThe arc-topology and descent for étale cohomologyMergedDevelops the excision theory of the arc-topology from valuation algebra and the scheme arc-site, and proves that a finitary v-sheaf with values in an infinity-category compactly generated by cotruncated objects is an arc-sheaf exactly when it satisfies Milnor excision. Arc-covers are the universal spectral submersions, are stable under cofiltered limits with affine transition maps, and on quasi-compact quasi-separated perfect schemes in characteristic p are exactly the universally effective epimorphisms; the coherent arc-topos gives excision and formal glueing for every arc-sheaf with values in a category with small limits. Applications include étale objects, characteristic-zero differential cohomology, perfect complexes on perfections, completed generic fibres and integral perfectoid hyperdescent, and the end theorem is Artin–Grothendieck vanishing for arbitrary torsion coefficients on affinoid algebras.

Also cited in the evidence for #81 The prismatic site and prismatic cohomology.

ArithmeticDynamicsArithmetic dynamicsMergedDevelops the arithmetic of iterated maps, especially rational maps of the projective line: preperiodic points, canonical heights, local escape functions and invariant measures, dynatomic curves, and Galois actions on trees of iterated preimages. Main results include equidistribution of small points and of periodic points, the facts that z² + c over ℚ has at most one rational 3-cycle and no rational 4- or 5-cycles, Stoll's surjective arboreal representations, Ritt's classification of polynomials with equal iterates, uniform common torsion images for Legendre curves, and dynamical Mordell–Lang for étale maps.

Also cited in the evidence for #95 Dynamical degree of a dominant rational self-map.

ArithmeticFuchsianGroupsArithmetic Fuchsian groups, commensurators and isospectral coversMergedDevelops the arithmetic theory of lattices in PSL₂(ℝ) coming from orders in quaternion algebras over totally real fields split at exactly one real place. Main results are discreteness, finite covolume and the covolume formula for maximal orders, the finiteness theorems of Borel for bounded covolume and of Takeuchi for a given signature, Margulis' criterion that a lattice is arithmetic exactly when its commensurator is dense, and nonisometric isospectral degree-eight covers of every closed hyperbolic surface of genus at least three with nonarithmetic maximal uniformizing group.

It also provides symmetric and other powers of representations and the conditions on residual images used in automorphy lifting.

ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpacesPartIIArithmetic locally symmetric spaces, part II — weight multiplicitiesMergedProves fixed-level bounds in the weight aspect for the cohomology of congruence subgroups of SL₂ over a number field with a complex place, using completed homology and Iwasawa modules. AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsGalois representations attached to modular and Hilbert modular formsMergedRealises the Galois representations of cuspidal eigenforms of weight at least two in parabolic cohomology and compares them with the motivic construction, constructs those of Hilbert modular forms, and places the Artin representations of weight-one forms in the same normalisation. AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartIIAutomorphic Galois representations, part II — regular algebraic GLₙMergedConstructs the continuous semisimple Galois representations attached to regular algebraic cuspidal representations of GLₙ over CM and totally real fields, through the cohomology of compact unitary Shimura varieties in the polarized case and through boundary cohomology in the non-self-dual case. Main results are local–global compatibility away from the coefficient prime, comparison at the coefficient prime, partly under an irreducibility hypothesis, assembly into compatible systems, and residual properties such as the existence of infinitely many decomposed generic primes.

AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactorsPartIIAutomorphic L-functions and local factors, part IIMergedDevelops the doubling method for standard L-functions: classical doubling for unitary groups, with its archimedean sections, and generalised doubling for split symplectic, special orthogonal and general spin groups. BallQuotientsAndComplexHyperbolicLatticesBall quotients and complex hyperbolic latticesMergedStudies the complex ball as the symmetric space of PU(1,n) and the lattices in this group, arithmetic or not: cusps, finite generation, Selberg's lemma, and the quotient of the ball as a normal complex space. It constructs the toroidal and minimal compactifications and proves that the minimal compactification is a normal projective variety, so that every ball quotient is quasi-projective, and that the quotient by a torsion-free lattice has a unique algebraic structure; in the arithmetic case it compares with the Baily–Borel compactification. It ends with totally geodesic and special subvarieties.

BochnerSpacesBochner spacesMergedDevelops measurability, Bochner and Pettis integration, Bochner spaces, integral operators and vector measures for functions with values in a Banach space. BorelEquivalenceRelationsBorel equivalence relations, the G₀ dichotomy and Silver's theoremMergedDevelops the classical descriptive set theory of Borel equivalence relations on Polish and standard Borel spaces by category methods, without forcing or effective descriptive set theory. Its two main results are Silver's theorem, that a Borel equivalence relation has countably many classes or continuum many pairwise inequivalent points forming a Cantor set, and the dichotomy of Kechris, Solecki and Todorcevic, that an analytic graph has a countable Borel colouring or receives a continuous homomorphism from the graph G₀; reusable tools include the perfect set theorem for analytic sets, Borel colourings and the Kuratowski–Ulam theorem.

Also cited in the evidence for #94 Borel complete class of countable structures.

CalibratedGeometryCalibrations, volume minimization and special Lagrangian geometryMergedDevelops calibrated geometry, from the linear algebra of comass and calibrated planes through volume minimization to the Kähler and special Lagrangian calibrations and the deformation theory of compact special Lagrangian submanifolds. Main results are the fundamental theorem of calibrations for compact submanifolds and integral chains, the Wirtinger inequality in every degree with volume minimization of complex submanifolds of Kähler manifolds, minimality of special Lagrangian submanifolds, and McLean's theorem that their deformations form a smooth manifold of dimension equal to the first Betti number.

CharacteristicClassesCharacteristic classes and Chern–Weil theoryMergedBuilds characteristic classes of vector bundles both topologically, from Thom classes and the cohomology of projective bundles, and geometrically, from the curvature of a connection, and proves that the two agree in real cohomology. It constructs the Stiefel–Whitney classes mod 2, the integral Chern, Pontryagin and Euler classes with Thom and Gysin maps, the cohomology of classifying spaces, the Chern character and the Todd, L and Â classes. Main results include the vanishing of the Euler class as the obstruction to a nowhere-zero section, the Euler class of a closed manifold evaluating to its Euler characteristic, Thom's theorem on embedded representatives in codimensions one and two, Whitney's nonimmersion theorem for projective spaces, Borel's theorem on the rational cohomology of classifying spaces and the identification of the second Stiefel–Whitney class with the obstruction to a spin structure.

ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocityPartIIComplex multiplication and explicit reciprocity, part IIMergedExtends the theory of complex multiplication to arithmetic heights of CM abelian varieties. With a height bound for CM lifts this gives finiteness of CM points of bounded degree, while the individual Colmez conjecture is only formulated.

DiracScatteringAndNonlinearFourierTransformDirac scattering and the non-linear Fourier transformMergedDevelops one-dimensional Dirac systems on the half-line: fundamental solutions, Hermite–Biehler functions, scattering functions and, for square-integrable potentials, the SU(1,1) non-linear Fourier transform with its Plancherel identity. It continues with de Branges spaces, Weyl functions and spectral measures, and, for real potentials, proves pointwise convergence of the non-linear Fourier transform at almost every resonance-free point, the conditional form of Poltoratski's non-linear Carleson theorem.
  • #43 SU(1,1) non-linear Fourier transform of an L2 function on the half-line: §§5.1–5.5 (scattering limit, q⌃, convergence in Π-measure), on Layers 1–4 (§§1.1–1.5 and most of §1.6 in code; of Layer 2, §2.1, most of §2.2 and the maximal bound of §2.3 in code)
  • #44 Scattering functions of a real Dirac system: §§1.1–1.6 (Carathéodory with a holomorphic parameter; §§1.1–1.5, and §1.6 apart from the derivative formula, in code), §§3.1–3.3 (Dirac system, E, Ẽ), §4.1 (a, b), §4.3
DynamicalDegreesOfRationalMapsDynamical degrees of rational mapsMergedBuilds the degree theory of dominant rational maps between projective varieties over an arbitrary field: the graph of a rational map, intersection numbers, and the positivity inequalities of Siu and of Khovanskii and Teissier. Its main results are the existence of the dynamical degrees as limits, their independence of the polarisation, their log-concavity and their invariance under birational conjugacy; the algebraic entropy is defined from them.
  • #95 Dynamical degree of a dominant rational self-map: §1.2 (graph, in code: Scheme.RationalMap.graph), §4.1 (mixedDegree), §4.4, Layer 5 (dynamicalDegree)
EllipticCurveModularityModularity and modular parametrisations of elliptic curves over ℚMergedBuilds the correspondence between elliptic curves over ℚ and weight-two newforms with rational coefficients, and the modular parametrisation of a curve by the modular curve X₀(N) at its conductor. Main results are the existence and uniqueness of the newform attached to every elliptic curve over ℚ, deduced from Serre's modularity theorem and Faltings's isogeny theorem, with level equal to the conductor and agreement of all Euler factors, and the equivalence of the newform, Jacobian-quotient and parametrisation forms of modularity, with analytic continuation of the L-function.
  • #71 Abelian variety Af attached to a weight-2 newform: (A_F⁰)
EllipticCurveModularityImaginaryQuadraticPartIIModularity of elliptic curves over imaginary quadratic fields, Part II: all CM fieldsMergedProves that every elliptic curve over every CM number field is modular: it corresponds to a weight-zero isobaric automorphic representation of GL₂ with trivial central character, with matching local factors at every finite place and matching L-functions. It adapts the prime-switching argument used for imaginary quadratic fields by making the auxiliary curve modular before cyclotomic base change and, for fields containing a fifth root of unity, by a modularity lifting theorem at 5 that keeps a place with unipotent residual inertia, produced by degenerating the family of covers.

Also cited in the evidence for #72 CM automorphic representation.

EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizationsPeriods of elliptic curves and modular parametrizationsMergedDevelops the invariants of an elliptic curve over a number field built from the period lattices at its infinite places: local and global periods, the analytic order of the Tate–Shafarevich group defined through the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer formula, and lattice formulas for the Faltings height. For curves over ℚ it treats optimal modular parametrizations, the Petersson formula for the modular degree, divisibility results for the Manin constant, rationality of L(E,1)/Ω in analytic rank zero, and Stevens' curve of least Faltings height in an isogeny class.

Also cited in the evidence for #71 Abelian variety Af attached to a weight-2 newform, #85 Stable Faltings height of an abelian variety.

EndoscopicTransferAndUnitaryTraceComparisonEndoscopic transfer and unitary trace comparisonMergedBuilds the theory of stable conjugacy and endoscopic transfer: transfer factors, the fundamental lemma, and the stable and twisted trace comparisons for unitary groups. It uses them to stabilise the traces of Igusa correspondences, to describe local packets and the global classification for unitary groups, and to prove transfers in rank four such as the one between GSp₄ and GL₄.

Also cited in the evidence for #5 Infinitesimal character (and Casimir eigenvalue) of a representation of a real reductive group, #7 Discrete series representations of GL2(ℝ), #11 Split classical groups Sp2n, SOl and general spin groups GSpinl, #82 The quasi-split unitary group of a self-dual hermitian lattice, #88 Weak functorial transfer to GLN.

EnriquesSurfacesEnriques surfaces, projective models and automorphismsMergedConstructs the algebraic geometry of Enriques surfaces: integral Picard and numerical invariants, genus-one pencils, linear systems of low degree, double-plane and Reye models, and automorphism groups through their action on the associated lattices. Its main results identify the automorphism group of a generic nodal or unnodal Enriques surface, away from small characteristics, with a level-two congruence subgroup of a reflection group acting on such a lattice.
  • #19 Enriques surface over an algebraically closed field

Also cited in the evidence for #20 Families of Enriques surfaces and the moduli stack ℳEnr.

EnriquesSurfacesAndIntegralNonexistenceEnriques surfaces and nonexistence over the integersMergedTreats the arithmetic of Enriques surfaces in all characteristics, including two: genus-one fibrations, canonical coverings, families and Picard descent. Its main results are that there is no flat proper family of Enriques surfaces over the integers, and that there is no non-exceptional Enriques surface over the field with two elements whose Picard scheme is constant.
  • #19 Enriques surface over an algebraically closed field: Layer 0
  • #20 Families of Enriques surfaces and the moduli stack ℳEnr: §2.1
EtaleCohomologyFoundationsÉtale cohomology foundationsMergedBuilds étale cohomology of schemes with finite, adic and rational coefficients: Nagata compactification, constructible sheaves, proper and smooth base change, cohomological dimension, Artin vanishing and finiteness, purity, duality and perverse sheaves. Its main results are the six operations on bounded constructible complexes with their base change, Künneth and duality theorems, and the exact sequence of arithmetic fundamental groups.
  • #18 Cup product in étale cohomology: §F.1, §0.9 (ShiftedHom cup product; schemes)
  • #22 Galois action on the étale cohomology of the geometric fibre: §F.4.6
  • #84 Selmer sections and the localisation map: §F.0.A2
EtaleDualityAndPerverseSheavesÉtale cohomology foundations, part II: refined duality, perverse decomposition and local tracesMergedExtends the foundations of étale cohomology of schemes with duality for constructible complexes over self-injective finite coefficients, flat traces with multiplicities, higher étale Chern classes, and the functor calculus of perverse sheaves and intersection complexes. Main results are the weight filtration and geometric semisimplicity of mixed perverse sheaves, the decomposition theorem and relative hard Lefschetz, and trace comparisons for cohomological correspondences in the contracting and tubular-neighbourhood cases.

Also cited in the evidence for #18 Cup product in étale cohomology.

EulerSystemsAndKolyvaginSystemsEuler systems, Kolyvagin systems and higher-rank descentMergedDevelops the passage from Euler systems of Galois cohomology classes to bounds on Selmer groups: Kolyvagin derivative classes, Kolyvagin systems of rank one, descent with bounded error, self-dual Selmer structures, Stark systems and higher-rank systems, and Iwasawa-theoretic variation. Main results are Selmer bounds from a nonzero Kolyvagin class after Rubin, Mazur–Rubin and Howard, with equality under primitivity, Fitting-ideal control from higher-rank Euler systems, and Rubin's weak Leopoldt theorem. Arithmetic Euler systems and their norm relations enter as hypotheses.

Also cited in the evidence for #10 Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg antisymmetrisation map.

FaltingsFinitenessAndIsogenyTheoremsFaltings finiteness, semisimplicity and isogeny theoremsMergedProves the finiteness theorems of Faltings' 1983 paper on abelian varieties over number fields, with the corrections of his erratum. Main results are finiteness of principally polarized abelian varieties of bounded modular height, stationarity of heights in ℓ-divisible towers, the Tate conjecture for endomorphisms and semisimplicity of Tate modules, finiteness of isogeny classes with good reduction outside a finite set, the Shafarevich conjecture for abelian varieties and for curves of genus at least two, and the corresponding statements for elliptic curves.

Also cited in the evidence for #85 Stable Faltings height of an abelian variety.

FaltingsFinitenessAndIsogenyTheoremsPartIIQuantitative isogenies and uniform integral Tate boundsMergedDevelops quantitative forms of Faltings' isogeny theory for abelian varieties over number fields and finitely generated fields of characteristic zero. Rosati metrics and the Masser–Wüstholz period theorem give polynomial bounds on endomorphisms, arithmetic of orders and Hom discriminants removes polarizations from isogeny estimates, and an elliptic isogeny estimate becomes a bound in the height of the j-invariant. Main results are quantitative simple factorization, isogeny bounds depending only on the dimension, and uniform index bounds for integral Tate algebras and adelic Galois images.

Also cited in the evidence for #85 Stable Faltings height of an abelian variety.

FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheoryFinite flat group schemes and integral p-adic Hodge theoryMergedDevelops finite flat commutative group schemes and p-divisible groups, with the integral p-adic Hodge theory classifying them by semilinear algebra: Tate's Hodge–Tate decomposition and generic fibre theorem, Raynaud's results and the Oort–Tate classification, Dieudonné theory with the Dieudonné–Manin classification, Fontaine–Laffaille theory, and Breuil–Kisin theory, including Kisin's classification of crystalline lattices and of p-divisible groups over p-adic integer rings. Applications include local residual types and Serre weights, Fontaine's ramification bound, flat deformation conditions and Raynaud's determinant theorem.
  • #21 Fppf cohomology with supports in a closed subset

Also cited in the evidence for #20 Families of Enriques surfaces and the moduli stack ℳEnr.

FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheoryPartIIFinite flat group schemes and integral p-adic Hodge theory, part IIMergedBuilds integral tensor structures on Dieudonné displays and the deformation theory of p-divisible groups with tensors. Main results are the criteria for very good integral Hodge embeddings, the theorem identifying adapted deformations over discrete valuation rings with the local-model quotient of a versal family under an infinitesimal compatibility hypothesis, and the construction of adapted ordinary lifts from a central Newton–Levi filtration, to which every element of the rational σ-centralizer lifts as a self-quasi-isogeny.

Also cited in the evidence for #21 Fppf cohomology with supports in a closed subset.

FourManifoldsFour-dimensional manifolds, intersection forms and elliptic examplesMergedBuilds the geometry and algebra of four-manifolds: explicit smooth models and handle decompositions, the homotopy classification by intersection forms, topological surgery and the homeomorphism classification, Spinᶜ structures, and elliptic surfaces. It includes the restrictions on definite intersection forms that come from anti-self-dual gauge theory.

FreeGroupsAndLimitGroupsFree groups, equations over groups, and limit groupsMergedDevelops free groups and the groups that resemble them: conjugacy, roots and centralizers, Stallings graphs of finitely generated subgroups, Bass–Serre theory, equations and coordinate groups over a group, quadratic and Makanin's generalized equations, limit groups, Lyndon length functions and JSJ decompositions. Main results are decidability of systems of equations and inequations over a free group, Merzljakov's theorem on formal solutions, the equivalence of limit groups, fully residually free groups and coordinate groups of irreducible algebraic sets, finite Makanin–Razborov diagrams, and the Kharlampovich–Myasnikov implicit function theorems.

FukayaTheoryFukaya categories of compact Lagrangian branesMergedConstructs Lagrangian Floer cohomology and the filtered curved Fukaya category of compact Lagrangian branes in a symplectic manifold, with weak bounding cochains, continuation functors, modules and split-closed derived categories, built from moduli spaces of pseudoholomorphic discs. Main results are the curved A∞ relations, invariance of the localized categories under Hamiltonian isotopy, the bound of the number of intersection points of an exact or weakly exact brane with its Hamiltonian image by its total Betti number, and the Floer cohomology of the zero section.

GaloisActionOnEtaleCohomologyThe Galois action on étale cohomology of geometric fibresMergedConstructs the action of the absolute Galois group on the étale cohomology of the geometric fibre, for a scheme over a field and for a rigid space over a complete non-archimedean field. GaugeQuiltFieldTheoryConnected symplectic gauge field theory and quilted sewingMergedConstructs the connected (2+1)-dimensional Floer field theory of Wehrheim and Woodward for coprime rank and degree. Surfaces carrying unitary bundles with fixed determinant are sent to integral, ℤ/4-graded extended Fukaya categories of their moduli spaces of central-curvature connections, and three-dimensional bordisms to quilted A∞ functors. Main results are the Lagrangian correspondences of elementary bordisms, independence of the Cerf decomposition, and the resulting functor on the decorated connected bordism category.

GaussianIsoperimetryAndClustersWeighted perimeter, Gaussian isoperimetry and clustersMergedBuilds perimeter with respect to a positive density on Euclidean space, clusters of sets with their interfaces and isoperimetric profiles, and Ehrhard symmetrisation. Main results are the Gaussian isoperimetric theorem with its equality case (half-spaces are the only minimisers), also in its two-cell cluster form, the inequalities of Borell–Sudakov–Tsirelson and Bobkov, and Ehrhard's inequality for convex sets.

GeometricCurrentsIntegral currents, geometric homology and minimizing hypersurfacesMergedBuilds currents on Riemannian manifolds: mass, integer-rectifiable and integral currents, boundary, pushforward and slicing, and the homology of integral currents with its comparison to singular homology. It then proves deformation and compactness theorems, treats the Plateau problem and mass minimisation in a homology class, and proves interior regularity of area-minimising hypersurfaces in ambient dimension at most seven.

GeometricMeasureTheoryGeometric measure theory: rectifiability, BV, and finite perimeterMergedBuilds Euclidean geometric measure theory: rectifiable curves and arclength, rectifiable sets with the area and coarea formulas, functions of bounded variation and their variation measures, approximation and compactness, and sets of finite perimeter with their reduced boundaries. Main results are the structure theorem for sets of finite perimeter, the coarea formula for functions of bounded variation, traces and the Gauss–Green formula, giving the measure-theoretic basis used for the Euclidean isoperimetric inequality.

GeometricTopologyGeometric topologyMergedBuilds the geometric topology of manifolds from the smooth level upwards: manifolds with boundary, gluing, handles and tubular neighbourhoods, locally flat embeddings, diffeomorphism groups, knots and links, Dehn surgery, hyperbolic structures, decompositions of three-manifolds, Heegaard splittings, foliations and triangulations. GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransferGL₂ automorphic representations and transferMergedBuilds the local and adelic representation theory of GL₂ that links classical modular forms to automorphic transfer: newvectors, Whittaker functions and local factors, the comparison with classical and Hilbert modular forms, the Jacquet–Langlands correspondence, and cyclic and solvable base change. GromovWittenTheoryGromov–Witten theory of closed symplectic manifoldsMergedConstructs the cohomology-valued Gromov–Witten operations of a closed symplectic manifold with a tame almost complex structure from virtual classes of moduli spaces of stable maps, and from them the Novikov-completed cohomological field theory, the genus-zero quantum product and descendant and ancestor classes. Main results are the Kontsevich–Manin axioms for these operations, independence of the almost complex structure, the genus-one degree-zero normalization by the Euler characteristic, associativity of the quantum product, and the string, dilaton, divisor and genus-zero topological recursion relations.

GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfacesFinite group actions on compact Riemann surfacesMergedDevelops finite group actions on compact Riemann surfaces of genus at least two through generating vectors of a signature, with refined passports, braid and topological equivalence, full automorphism groups of families, hyperelliptic and cyclic trigonal families, and the action on differentials and on the Jacobian. Main results are the Riemann existence criterion relating actions to generating vectors, the Hurwitz and Wiman bounds with Harvey's criterion for cyclic groups, Singerman's list, Macbeath's fixed-point formula, the Eichler trace and Chevalley–Weil formulas, and the group-algebra decomposition of the Jacobian.

HeegaardFloerAnalytic Heegaard Floer homologyMergedBuilds integral Morse homology, the Fredholm and Cauchy–Riemann analysis of pseudoholomorphic curves with compactness and gluing, Lagrangian Floer homology in the exact case, and Heegaard Floer homology defined by holomorphic curves. HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurvesHilbert modular varieties and quaternionic Shimura curvesMergedConstructs Hilbert modular varieties of a totally real field with Deligne–Pappas integral models, and Shimura curves attached to quaternion algebras split at exactly one real place, with the Drinfeld half-plane, integral model towers, cohomology and Hecke actions. IgusaVarietiesAndTorsionConcentrationIgusa varieties, compactified period fibres and torsion concentrationMergedProves Koshikawa's vanishing theorem for unitary Shimura varieties: at a prime split completely in the CM field, with hyperspecial level, localization at a generic maximal ideal of the spherical Hecke algebra confines mod-ℓ cohomology to one side of the middle degree, with integral and boundary consequences (Caraiani–Newton in the form needed when F⁺ = ℚ). The route is Mantovan's filtration through Igusa varieties and local Shimura varieties, Koshikawa's semiperversity theorem for the local factor, and partial toroidal and minimal compactifications of central leaves and Igusa varieties identified with the fibres of the Hodge–Tate period map. Layer 0 sets up the quasi-split unitary similitude datum over a CM field with a self-dual lattice, its Hecke algebras, p-divisible groups with extra structure, Rapoport–Zink spaces and central leaves.

ImplicitAtlasesImplicit atlases and rational virtual fundamental classesMergedDevelops Pardon's implicit atlases on compact Hausdorff spaces, built from finite-group equivariant thickenings with obstruction spaces, and constructs from them virtual cochain complexes, a virtual orientation sheaf and a rational virtual fundamental class on Čech cohomology. Main results are independence of the class from the finite subatlas, its boundary, cobordism, refinement, pushforward and normalization properties, agreement with the Euler pairing of a smooth section, a product formula, and a formula for transverse fiber products through a Gysin map.

Also cited in the evidence for #24 Moduli stack of stable maps ℳg,n(X,β), #27 Perfect obstruction theory and virtual fundamental class.

IncompressibleFluidsIncompressible fluidsMergedDevelops the theory of the incompressible Navier–Stokes and Euler equations on the whole space: solenoidal fields, the Helmholtz–Weyl decomposition and the Leray projection, the Stokes semigroup and its estimates, and the nonlinear and compactness estimates. Main results include Leray's existence theorem for weak solutions, two-dimensional uniqueness and regularity, Prodi–Serrin weak–strong uniqueness, the Fujita–Kato and Kato theorems for mild solutions, and for the Euler equations Kato's local well-posedness, the Beale–Kato–Majda criterion, the inviscid limit and the global two-dimensional theory of Wolibner and Yudovich.

Also cited in the evidence for #33 Domain of dependence and development of initial data for the compressible Euler equations.

InfinitaryModelExistenceInfinitary model existence, omitting types and interpolationMergedDerives the model theory of the infinitary logic Lω₁ω over countable languages from one engine, the model existence theorem for consistency properties, together with countable fragments, infinitary types, projective classes and the graph translation from function symbols to relations. Main results are downward Löwenheim–Skolem for countable fragments, the omitting types theorem, Craig, Lyndon and Malitz interpolation and projective-class separation for sentences, and Marker's boundedness theorems showing that no Lω₁ω sentence defines the class of well-orders.

InstantonFloerTheoryInstanton Floer theoryMergedConstructs instanton Floer homology of three-manifolds from moduli spaces of anti-self-dual connections on cylinders, in three versions: an integral theory for admissible SO(3) bundles, a theory of integral homology spheres that accounts for the reducible connection, and a framed theory over the complex numbers for every closed oriented three-manifold. It establishes naturality, maps induced by cobordisms, operations from universal classes, exact surgery triangles, excision, Künneth formulas for disjoint unions, and an Atiyah–Floer type comparison in the admissible case.

Also cited in the evidence for #37 Caloric gauge and the manifold of caloric connections, #48 Topological class (instanton number) of a finite-energy connection on ℝ4.

IntegralHeckeAndGaloisDeterminantsIntegral Hecke actions, determinants and interpolationMergedDevelops the integral algebra that attaches Galois data to Hecke eigensystems over torsion and nonreduced coefficient rings: Chenevier's determinants as multiplicative polynomial laws, reconstruction of representations over henselian local rings, derived Hecke actions on cohomology, and Hecke polynomials for GLₙ and GSp₄. Main results are an interpolation theorem giving a unique continuous determinant with prescribed Frobenius characteristic polynomials, its descent modulo a nilpotent ideal with a uniform exponent, and an integral Ribet theorem producing an extension module whose Fitting ideal lies in the congruence ideal.

Also cited in the evidence for #69 Galois representation attached to a cuspidal Hecke eigenform, #89 ℓ-adic Galois representation with values in the Mumford–Tate group.

InverseGaloisAndArithmeticFundamentalGroupsInverse Galois theory and arithmetic fundamental groupsMergedDevelops the arithmetic of finite covers behind inverse Galois theory: Galois algebras, local systems, pro-étale and tame fundamental groups, rigidity, braid orbits of Nielsen classes, embedding problems and Hurwitz spaces. Main results are Hilbert's irreducibility theorem over number fields, rational rigidity, the stable classification of braid orbits by the lifting invariant, Shafarevich's theorem that every finite solvable group is a Galois group over a number field, and the description of components of Hurwitz spaces by lifting invariants. The inverse Galois problem over ℚ is formulated as an open statement.

Also cited in the evidence for #8 Glauberman correspondence, #20 Families of Enriques surfaces and the moduli stack ℳEnr, #84 Selmer sections and the localisation map.

InverseGaloisAndArithmeticFundamentalGroupsPartIIInverse Galois theory and arithmetic fundamental groups, Part II: homological stability for Hurwitz spacesMergedProves the homological stability theorem of Ellenberg, Venkatesh and Westerland for Hurwitz spaces of branched covers of the disc with monodromy in a nonsplitting conjugacy class of a finite group. The components of all Hurwitz spaces form a graded ring whose central stabilizing element induces homology isomorphisms in a linear range, proved with Koszul-type complexes and arc complexes. Consequences are Betti number bounds uniform in the number of branch points, transferred to the étale cohomology of Hurwitz schemes over the algebraic closure of a finite field.

Also cited in the evidence for #59 Mapping class group of a punctured surface.

JacobianChallengeThe Jacobian challengeMergedFor a smooth proper geometrically connected curve over a field, builds the Jacobian as an abelian variety representing the degree-zero Picard functor, without assuming a rational point, and, for each rational point, the Abel–Jacobi map with its universal property. Along the way it develops Riemann–Roch and Serre duality on curves, relative Picard schemes, and abelian varieties with their duals, polarizations and theta divisor.
  • #30 Theta divisor of a Jacobian: §§C.4, D.5, E.5
  • #84 Selmer sections and the localisation map: Layer F
JacobianChallengePartIIThe Jacobian challenge, Part II — relative Jacobians, theta bundles and universal differencesMergedContinues the Jacobian challenge in families: for a smooth projective curve over a noetherian base it shows that the relative Jacobian is an abelian scheme with a canonical principal polarization, and builds Abel maps without a chosen section. It then treats theta divisors and the Poincaré bundle for an arbitrary divisor of degree one, and the difference and Faltings–Zhang morphisms on fibre powers.
  • #30 Theta divisor of a Jacobian: §§3.2–3.3, 3.10–3.13

K3SurfacesK3 surfaces, cohomology, periods, cones and TorelliMergedDevelops the geometry of algebraic and complex analytic K3 surfaces: the absence of global vector fields and one-forms in characteristic p > 2, Ricci-flat Kähler metrics, the even unimodular lattice of signature (3,19) on second cohomology, marked deformation families, period domains and twistor families. Main results are local and global Torelli theorems with surjectivity of the period map, the description of the Kähler cone by smooth rational curves, Picard number 22 for unirational K3 surfaces, and the period description of K3 surfaces with a fixed-point-free involution.

Also cited in the evidence for #19 Enriques surface over an algebraically closed field.

K3SurfacesAndSymplecticBoundednessK3 surfaces, Part II: Picard lattices and symplectic boundednessMergedExtends the theory of K3 surfaces to smooth proper surfaces over arbitrary fields of every characteristic, with their crystalline and étale cohomology and their geometric and arithmetic Picard lattices. Main results are the vanishing of global vector fields and one-forms in every characteristic, bounds on Picard ranks, faithful action of automorphisms on cohomology, a uniform bound on finite automorphism groups, Mukai's bound of 960 for finite symplectic groups in characteristic zero, and separated moduli of polarized K3 surfaces of fixed degree with finitely many isomorphism classes over each finite field.

Also cited in the evidence for #19 Enriques surface over an algebraically closed field, #20 Families of Enriques surfaces and the moduli stack ℳEnr.

KleinianGroupsKleinian groups, hyperbolic 3-manifolds, and arithmetic volumeMergedDevelops discrete subgroups of PSL(2,ℂ) acting on hyperbolic 3-space, their fundamental domains and covolumes, and the Bianchi groups with their congruence subgroups. Main results compute from explicit fundamental domains the covolume of PSL(2,ℤ[i]) as one third of Catalan's constant and that of PSL(2,ℤ[ω]) as √3 L(2,χ₋₃)/8, and deduce the volumes of two congruence quotient manifolds. It also formulates Thurston's open question on rational relations among hyperbolic volumes, with a provable finite approximation.

Also cited in the evidence for #68 Arithmetic lattice in a semisimple Lie group.

KTheoryFiniteLocalFieldsK-theory of finite and local fieldsMergedComputes the higher algebraic K-groups of nonarchimedean local fields, building on Quillen's calculation for finite fields, through rigidity for henselian rings, integral K₂ and trace-method calculations of Hesselholt and Madsen with logarithmic de Rham–Witt complexes. Main results include Moore's structure theorem for K₂ of a local field, Izhboldin's torsion theorem in characteristic p, and the identification of the p-completed K-groups with Galois cohomology, keeping integral, finite-coefficient and completed groups distinct, together with comparison maps to completions of number fields.

Also cited in the evidence for #10 Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg antisymmetrisation map.

LieGroupsPartIILie groups, part II — minimal parabolic subgroups, the Bruhat decomposition and the self-adjoint matrix model of real reductive groupsMergedContinues the structure theory of real reductive Lie groups after the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions, for groups that need not be connected, semisimple, linear or of finite centre. It proves conjugacy of Cartan involutions and of Iwasawa decompositions with a common split centre, defines the minimal parabolic subgroup with its Langlands decomposition, proves the Bruhat decomposition, and shows that the real points of a connected reductive group over ℝ, realised as a self-adjoint matrix group, carry all of this structure.
  • #4 Parabolic subgroups of a real semisimple Lie group (minimal and standard parabolics): Layer 3
  • #79 Minimal parabolic subgroups and the Langlands decomposition (real rank one): Layer 3

Also cited in the evidence for #1 Real rank of a semisimple Lie group, #54 Character variety of a surface group in a real reductive group.

LocallyAnalyticDistributionsLocally analytic distributions, growth and character spacesMergedDevelops locally analytic functions and distributions on ℤₚ, on products of p-adic integer rings and on compact p-adic analytic manifolds, the Hyodo–Kato cohomology of semistable Stein curves, and the Fredholm theory of Serre, Coleman and Buzzard for compact operators over Noetherian Banach algebras, as needed for p-adic families. Main results include nonarchimedean Hahn–Banach and open mapping theorems, the Amice transform dictionary, the Amice–Vélu–Vishik extension and uniqueness theorem with its sharp degree bound, Mellin transforms on character spaces, and finite-slope decompositions.

Also cited in the evidence for #77 Emerton's completed cohomology of modular curves.

MaassFormsMaass forms: the hyperbolic Laplacian on Γ₀(N)\ℍ, Hecke–Maass newforms, and their invariantsMergedDevelops the classical theory of weight-zero Maass cusp forms for Γ₀(N) with a Dirichlet character: the hyperbolic Laplacian and its self-adjoint realization, Fourier–Whittaker expansions in K-Bessel functions, Hecke operators, Atkin–Lehner and Fricke involutions, and newforms with strong multiplicity one. Main results include Selberg's bound λ₁ ≥ 3/16, Selberg's eigenvalue conjecture as a statement proved at level one, Weyl's law, L-functions of Hecke–Maass newforms with their functional equation, and Maass forms for PSL₂(ℤ[i]) on hyperbolic three-space.

Also cited in the evidence for #35 Absolutely continuous spectrum of a self-adjoint operator, #40 The Laplace–Beltrami operator on L2 of a complete Riemannian manifold.

MathematicalBilliardsMathematical billiardsMergedBuilds the two classical planar billiard systems. For a strictly convex table it constructs the billiard ball map on the phase cylinder, shows that it is a twist map preserving the measure sin δ dδ ds, and proves Birkhoff's theorems on rotational invariant curves and on periodic orbits. For a polygonal table it constructs the phase space, the billiard flow and the translation surface obtained by unfolding a rational polygon, and for general measure-preserving flows it develops weak mixing.
  • #92 Billiard ball map of a convex billiard table: Layer 5, all of Def 92 for C^k tables in Layers 1–5
  • #93 Billiard flow in a polygon: §§7.1–8.3 (polygonal table, phase space, singular set, flow, Liouville measure), Layer 12 (weak mixing), Layers 9–11 (unfolding)

Also cited in the evidence for #39 Hard sphere dynamics on the torus.

MetaplecticAutomorphicFormsMetaplectic groups, Weil representations and automorphic theta kernelsMergedConstructs the Weil representation and the metaplectic double cover over local fields and adeles, and from them the local theta correspondence, global theta kernels and theta lifts, half-integral weight Maass forms with the Kohnen plus space, and the Shimura lift. Main results are Howe duality, conservation relations and see-saw identities, the regularized Siegel–Weil formula, the Rallis inner product formula and theta nonvanishing, the Duke–Imamoḡlu–Tóth trace identities, and the genus-two Jacobi–Eisenstein analysis of Bump–Friedberg–Hoffstein used for nonvanishing of quadratic twists.

Also cited in the evidence for #11 Split classical groups Sp2n, SOl and general spin groups GSpinl, #88 Weak functorial transfer to GLN.

ModularCharactersAndBlocksPartIIModular characters and blocks, Part II — the Glauberman correspondence and heights of charactersMergedContinues the theory of blocks in two directions. The first is the character theory of coprime actions, ending in the Glauberman correspondence and its degree divisibility. The second is the theory of heights: Brauer's theorem on heights, height zero characters, the Fong–Reynolds correspondence and Reynolds' theorem on blocks with a normal defect group, with the height zero and Alperin–McKay conjectures stated as properties of blocks.
  • #2 p-block (Brauer block) of a finite group
  • #3 Defect group of a block: Layers 7–8
  • #8 Glauberman correspondence: Layers 1–5 (canonical extension, IsGlaubermanFamily, degree divisibility)

ModularCurvesPartIIModular curves, Part II — uniformization, integral boundary and Hecke cohomologyMergedCompares arithmetic modular curves with analytic quotients of the upper half-plane, builds their integral compactifications and bad fibres, and constructs the Hecke action on their Jacobians and cohomology. Results include compact uniformisation with the fields of definition of the cusps, Ihara's theorems, and, for the abelian varieties attached to newforms of weight 2, the corresponding quotients of J₀(N), oldform factors and Abel–Jacobi maps.
  • #69 Galois representation attached to a cuspidal Hecke eigenform: §§14.1–14.4a, 14.11

ModularityAndLanglandsExtensionsModularity and Langlands extensionsMergedExtends modularity results to Artin representations, potential automorphy, symmetric power functoriality and the Sato–Tate conjecture, and treats packets for classical groups. It sets up the comparisons of normalizations these results need and states the remaining Langlands conjectures precisely.
  • #88 Weak functorial transfer to GLN: §1.6

ModularSymbolsPadicLFunctionsModular symbols and analytic p-adic L-functions of modular formsMergedBuilds the cohomological theory of modular symbols with arbitrary coefficients, with Hecke, diamond and sign operators, the cohomological Eichler–Shimura isomorphism and the rational Drinfeld–Manin splitting, and proves algebraicity of twisted critical L-values divided by signed periods. On the p-adic side it builds overconvergent modular symbols, proves Stevens' control theorem in small slope, and constructs the p-adic L-function of a refinement of slope less than k + 1 with its interpolation formula, uniqueness and integral ordinary form. It also covers the critical-slope p-adic L-function of a non-θ-critical refinement, Pollack's signed p-adic L-functions at supersingular primes with a_p = 0, tame twists, and worked examples of level 11.

Also cited in the evidence for #69 Galois representation attached to a cuspidal Hecke eigenform.

MordellLawrenceVenkateshThe Mordell conjecture after Lawrence and VenkateshMergedProves Faltings's theorem, that a curve of genus at least two over a number field has finitely many rational points, by the method of Lawrence and Venkatesh, and the finiteness of solutions of the S-unit equation by the same method. Along the way it builds p-adic period maps for families of abelian varieties, the Kodaira–Parshin family, and the surface topology and mapping class groups used to show that its monodromy is large.
  • #58 MCG-finite representation of a surface group: §5.9

MotivesAndAlgebraicCyclesAlgebraic cycles, pure and mixed motivesMergedBuilds Chow motives from correspondences with their Betti, de Rham and étale realizations, geometric motives with compact support from finite correspondences and motivic complexes, and Nori motives, and compares these constructions. Main results include Jannsen's semisimplicity theorem for numerical motives, comparisons with higher Chow groups, neutral and relative Tannakian reconstruction, and the identification of formal periods with the coordinate ring of a bitorsor. It formulates the Hodge, Tate, standard and Beilinson–Bloch conjectures as precise statements and proves their known implications and special cases.

Also cited in the evidence for #19 Enriques surface over an algebraically closed field, #95 Dynamical degree of a dominant rational self-map.

NashBlowupsNash blowupsMergedConstructs the Nash blowup of a variety as the closure of the smooth locus in the Grassmannian of rank-d quotients of the sheaf of differentials, together with the normalized Nash blowup and the iterations of both. It proves the universal property, Nobile's theorem that in characteristic zero the Nash blowup is an isomorphism only for smooth varieties, the theorem of Duarte and Núñez-Betancourt for normal varieties in characteristic p, and a principle by which iterated Nash blowups never resolve certain singularities.
  • #25 Nash blowup of a variety: §§1.2, 2.1–2.7 (nashBlowup)
  • #26 Normalized Nash blowup: §§6.1–6.4, 7.1–7.3 (normalizedNashBlowup)
NeronModelsAndSemistableAbelianVarietiesNéron models and semistable abelian varietiesMergedBuilds Néron models of abelian varieties over discrete valuation rings and Dedekind schemes, their special fibres and component groups, and semistable reduction, including the semistable reduction theorem and the uniformisation of degenerating abelian varieties. Consequences include the criterion of Néron–Ogg–Shafarevich, the monodromy pairing, and conductors and local Euler factors.

NoncompactQuantumSymmetryNoncompact quantum symmetry and particle representationsMergedDevelops the measurable and unitary representation theory of locally compact groups needed to pass from spacetime symmetry to particle models: rays and projective representations, locally continuous multipliers, smooth vectors, systems of imprimitivity and induction. The central results are the locally compact imprimitivity theorem with its normalized induced Hilbert-space model and the Mackey machine, which classifies regular irreducible representations of semidirect products through dual orbits, stabilizers and their projective representations. These are applied to the Euclidean, Galilei, Poincaré and de Sitter groups, with the domain and differentiability results that connect group representations to observables.

Also cited in the evidence for #1 Real rank of a semisimple Lie group, #4 Parabolic subgroups of a real semisimple Lie group (minimal and standard parabolics).

OddOrderTheoremThe Feit–Thompson odd order theoremMergedProves that every finite group of odd order is solvable, by the Bender–Glauberman local analysis and Peterfalvi's character theory. Along the way it develops the reduction to a minimal simple counterexample, local analysis for solvable groups of odd order, the Dade isometry and coherence of characters, the division of maximal subgroups of a minimal counterexample into five types with their character analysis, and the finite field comparison that yields the final contradiction.

Also cited in the evidence for #8 Glauberman correspondence.

OperatorKTheoryOperator K-theory, extensions and KK-theoryMergedBuilds the K-groups K₀ and K₁ of complex C*-algebras from projections and unitaries, and Kasparov's bivariant groups KK(A,B) with the Kasparov product. Main results are Bott periodicity, the six-term exact sequence of an extension, Atkinson's theorem and Fredholm indices, the Brown–Douglas–Fillmore theorem, K-theory computations for free-group, rotation and Cuntz algebras, the bootstrap class with the universal coefficient and Künneth theorems, and E-theory from asymptotic morphisms.

Also cited in the evidence for #36 Amenable trace on a C*-algebra.

OperatorTheory/SelfAdjointSpectralTheorySelf-adjoint spectral theory: the Borel functional calculus, unbounded operators, and Stone's theoremMergedDevelops the spectral theory of self-adjoint operators on Hilbert space: one-parameter unitary groups and their generators, the bounded Borel functional calculus and projection-valued measures, closed operators and their resolvents. Its last part constructs the spectral measure of an unbounded self-adjoint operator through the Cayley transform, proves the resolvent formula, and shows that the unitary group it generates by Yosida approximation is the one given by Stone's theorem.

OperatorTheory/UnboundedOperatorsUnbounded operators on Hilbert spaceMergedDevelops closed, densely defined operators on Hilbert space: the unbounded Borel functional calculus, normal operators and joint spectral theory, the essential spectrum, and the decomposition of a self-adjoint operator into pure point, absolutely continuous and singular continuous parts. It also covers symmetric operators and their self-adjoint extensions, closed forms and the Friedrichs extension, analytic vectors, and perturbation theorems of Kato–Rellich type.

PadicDifferentialEquationsAndRigidCohomologyp-adic differential equations, rigid cohomology and p-adic weightsMergedDevelops differential and Frobenius modules over the Robba ring and related rings of overconvergent series, and builds rigid cohomology of varieties over finite fields with coefficients in overconvergent F-isocrystals. Main results are Kedlaya's slope filtration theorem and the p-adic local monodromy theorem, finiteness, Poincaré duality and the Lefschetz trace formula for rigid cohomology with coefficients, Kedlaya's p-adic Weil II, and the equality of crystalline, rigid and ℓ-adic factors of zeta functions of smooth proper varieties over finite fields.

Also cited in the evidence for #22 Galois action on the étale cohomology of the geometric fibre.

PadicHodgeTheoryp-adic Hodge theory and geometric comparisonMergedBuilds the rational period rings and period functors of p-adic Hodge theory with their filtrations and admissibility, Robba realizations and B-pairs, monodromy, weak admissibility and Weil–Deligne parameters, and relative de Rham objects. Its endpoints are the group-valued period torsor, the localization presentation of the de Rham differential lattice, filtered de Rham comparison with coefficients, crystalline and semistable geometric comparison, and the good-reduction criterion. Explicit Kummer and Tate-curve periods fix the conventions.

Also cited in the evidence for #81 The prismatic site and prismatic cohomology.

PainleveEquationsPainlevé equationsMergedConstructs the Painlevé equations as flat nonlinear connections, the isomonodromic connections, on moduli spaces of meromorphic connections with prescribed singularities, and Painlevé functions as their horizontal sections. Main results are smoothness and symplectic structure of these phase spaces, flatness of the isomonodromic connection, the Painlevé property through the Riemann–Hilbert correspondence, Okamoto's surfaces of initial conditions with symmetries and Riccati reductions, tau functions from Fredholm determinant lines, and comparison with the classical scalar, Hamiltonian and linear-system forms.

PDEPartial differential equationsMergedBuilds the theory of second-order elliptic and parabolic equations on Euclidean domains: Sobolev spaces with their embedding, trace, extension and compactness theorems, the harmonic-analysis estimates (interpolation, Calderón–Zygmund theory, BMO), maximum principles, the Hopf lemma and the Aleksandrov–Bakelman–Pucci estimate, and the energy method for the Dirichlet, mixed and Neumann problems with their Fredholm theory and spectra. Its end results are solvability, regularity (De Giorgi–Nash–Moser, Schauder and Calderón–Zygmund estimates, Krylov–Safonov) and the maximum and Harnack principles for uniformly elliptic operators, with parabolic Schauder theory, Strichartz estimates and quasilinear existence as extensions.
  • #34 Neumann eigenvalues and eigenfunctions of the Laplacian on a bounded domain: §4.6 (with §§1.8, 4.5; §1.8 and §4.6 partly in code, §4.5 not started)
PELModuliSiegel and PEL moduli problemsMergedBuilds the moduli problems of abelian schemes with a polarization, an action of an order and a level structure, which include the Siegel, Hilbert and unitary cases. It starts from the linear algebra of lattices with an order action and their similitude groups, proves that the moduli problem is a smooth algebraic stack over the appropriate base, and identifies its complex points with a union of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains.

PerfectoidQuotientsPerfectoid quotients and their prismatic prerequisitesMergedDevelops the integral commutative algebra and explicit root constructions that accompany universal perfectoid quotients: Fontaine's generators over rings with torsion, torsion bounds for Witt vectors, removal of torsion, root closures, completed ring operations, radical formulas in characteristic p, and almost faithfully flat root extensions over a perfectoid field. Main results are that completions of root closures are perfectoid, an ind-syntomic refinement modulo p of André's cover, and functorial almost absolutely integrally closed extensions.

PerfectoidQuotientsPartIIPerfectoid quotients, part II: structure of integral perfectoid rings and the ind-syntomic André lemmaMergedExtends PerfectoidQuotients to the structure theory of integral perfectoid rings with torsion: what the tilt determines (the ring modulo ϖ^p, its torsion, adic completeness, being a valuation ring and its value group, algebraic closedness of the fraction field), fibre products along surjections, and presentations as quotients of torsion-free perfectoid rings. It proves André's lemma without completions, for faithfully flat ind-syntomic ϖ-Henselian extensions, and deduces faithfully flat semiperfectoid covers of all p-Zariski rings. For regular local rings it gives faithfully flat perfectoid covers and the almost vanishing of Tor along finite subextensions of the absolute integral closure, which for F-finite residue fields is Heitmann–Ma's theorem.

PositiveMassAsymptotically Euclidean geometry and positive massMergedDevelops asymptotically Euclidean ends of Riemannian manifolds, the ADM mass and weighted Sobolev and Hölder analysis on such manifolds, and gives two proofs of the Riemannian positive mass theorem with rigidity: the minimal hypersurface argument in dimensions 3 to 7 and Witten's spinor argument for spin manifolds in all dimensions. Main results are nonnegativity of the mass of every end and Euclidean rigidity at an end of zero mass, including the Cecchini–Zeidler form for one asymptotically Euclidean end of a complete spin manifold with arbitrary other ends.
