PotentialAutomorphyInfrastructurePartIIPotential automorphy infrastructure, Part II: polarized automorphy lifting and finiteness of deformation ringsMergedDevelops automorphy lifting for Galois representations of CM and totally real fields that are conjugate self-dual up to twist, using algebraic modular forms on definite unitary groups, their Hecke algebras and Taylor–Wiles patching. ProbabilisticAndMetricNumberTheoryPartIIProbabilistic, metric and ergodic number theory, Part II: transfer operators and uniform renewal countingMergedStudies a free semigroup of linear maps acting on a cone in n-dimensional space, n ≥ 3, through transfer operators for its countably many projective branches, developing the Ionescu-Tulcea–Marinescu inequality, Hennion's essential radius bound, Birkhoff cone contraction, a Ruelle–Perron–Frobenius theorem with spectral gap and a uniform renewal theorem. Main results are a unique exponent β(n) > 1 admitting a conformal probability measure and an asymptotic count of semigroup words by growth, uniform in the starting point, with β(3) = 2 and an explicit constant when n = 3.

ReductiveGroupsReductive algebraic groupsMergedBuilds the general theory of affine algebraic groups and reductive groups from Hopf algebras and comodules: representations, Lie algebras, quotients, tori, solvable and unipotent groups, structure theory with root data, parabolics and the Bruhat decomposition, finite groups of Lie type, and classification by root data. RelativisticQuantumFieldTheoryRelativistic quantum field theory, free fields and perturbative renormalizationMergedDevelops the mathematics linking Lorentzian field equations and Fock space models to local quantum fields: causal Green operators of the Klein–Gordon, Dirac and Maxwell equations, free fields as operator-valued tempered distributions, the Wightman and Haag–Kastler frameworks, Feynman graphs and their Hopf algebras, and renormalization by extension of distributions. Main results include the Reeh–Schlieder, spin–statistics and PCT theorems under stated hypotheses, Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction, Haag–Ruelle scattering and the LSZ formula for massive theories, the forest formula and Ward identities, and Shale's implementability theorem.

Also cited in the evidence for #33 Domain of dependence and development of initial data for the compressible Euler equations, #50 Lorentzian manifold, time orientation and spacetime, #51 Null (characteristic) hypersurface of a Lorentzian manifold, #99 Scaling limit of lattice φ4 fields (reachable random field).

RepresentationTheory/LieGroupsLie groups and the Lie algebra correspondenceMergedBuilds the correspondence between Lie groups and Lie algebras: the exponential map, the adjoint representation, the closed-subgroup theorem, the Lie functor and Lie's third theorem. It continues with the structure of compact and reductive groups: maximal tori and the Weyl integration formula, complexification and real forms, the Borel–Weil theorem, and the Cartan, Iwasawa and KAK decompositions with restricted roots.
  • #1 Real rank of a semisimple Lie group: L7, L9
  • #54 Character variety of a surface group in a real reductive group: L7, L9
  • #62 Riemannian symmetric space of a semisimple Lie group: L7, L9
  • #79 Minimal parabolic subgroups and the Langlands decomposition (real rank one): L9
RepresentationTheory/LieHighestWeightRepresentations of semisimple Lie algebras, highest weight theory, and the Weyl formulasMergedDevelops complements to the highest weight theory of semisimple Lie algebras, building on Verma modules, the Weyl character formula and the Harish-Chandra isomorphism. It proves that Serre's relations present a split semisimple Lie algebra in characteristic zero, constructs explicit models of the exceptional Lie algebras, among them G₂ and F₄ as derivation algebras of the octonions and of the Albert algebra, extends the comparisons of centres of enveloping algebras to reductive Lie algebras such as gl(n), and studies the affine Weyl group acting on the closed fundamental alcove.
  • #5 Infinitesimal character (and Casimir eigenvalue) of a representation of a real reductive group: §9.2
RepresentationTheory/ModularCharactersAndBlocksModular characters and blocks of finite groupsMergedDevelops the modular representation theory of a finite group at a prime p: p-modular systems, Brauer characters and the decomposition map, projective characters and Brauer reciprocity, blocks over the three coefficient rings, the Brauer homomorphism and defect groups. Its main results are Brauer's three main theorems, the conjugacy of defect groups, and the behaviour of blocks under normal subgroups.
  • #2 p-block (Brauer block) of a finite group: §§1.1, 1.3 (splitting p-modular system, lifting projectives; lifting idempotents in code), §§4.2–4.3 (Irr(B), IBr(B), SameBlock, principal block; SameBlock on complex characters and the principal block idempotent in code)
  • #3 Defect group of a block: §6.1 (relative traces, brauerHom with its kernel and Rosenberg's lemma in code), §6.2 (IsDefectGroup and the conjugacy of defect groups in code), §7.1 (brauer_first_main)
  • #9 Height zero characters of a block: §§4.2, 6.2, 7.1
RepresentationTheory/SpinRepresentationsClifford algebras, the Pin and Spin groups, and spin representationsMergedDevelops Clifford algebras of quadratic spaces, the Pin and Spin double covers of orthogonal groups, and spin and half-spin representations, over the complex numbers and for real forms. Main results are a criterion for Clifford algebra automorphisms to come from isometries, highest-weight descriptions of the spin and half-spin representations, the vector representation in dimension six as an exterior square, comparisons of real and complex spin groups, triality for Spin₈, and Kostant's isotypy theorem and ρ-decomposition for the Clifford algebra of a semisimple Lie algebra.

Also cited in the evidence for #64 Spinc structure on an oriented manifold.

RiemannHypothesisForDirichletLFunctions/FiniteReductiveGroupsFinite reductive groups and the characters of GL_n(F_q)MergedBuilds the complex character theory of the finite groups of rational points of connected reductive groups over finite fields: Harish-Chandra induction and series, Gelfand–Graev representations, and Deligne–Lusztig induction through ℓ-adic cohomology, with the inner product formula, Green functions and Lusztig series. For GL_n it gives Green's classification of the irreducible characters, Shintani descent in prime degree, and the depth-zero supercuspidal representations of p-adic GL_n.

Also cited in the evidence for #2 p-block (Brauer block) of a finite group, #8 Glauberman correspondence.

RiemannHypothesisForDirichletLFunctions/HermitianFormsAndLatticesHermitian forms and latticesMergedDevelops the arithmetic of Hermitian and skew-Hermitian forms over quadratic étale algebras and of their lattices: unitary group schemes and their reductive structure, Witt theory and the local and global classification of Hermitian spaces, and Hermitian lattices over rings of integers with their duals, integral models, genera, class sets and masses. Its main results are the Tamagawa number two of unitary groups, the mass formula, Siegel's main theorem and Hermitian theta series of definite lattices.
  • #82 The quasi-split unitary group of a self-dual hermitian lattice: §4.6
RiemannianGeometryCurvature, Jacobi fields, submanifolds, and Bochner geometryMergedDevelops Riemannian geometry from curvature onwards: Jacobi fields and index forms, comparison theorems and space forms, isometric immersions with the first and second variation of volume, focal points, and Bochner–Weitzenböck formulas. It also treats Schrödinger-type operators on compact manifolds.
  • #31 Totally geodesic subvariety of a ball quotient: R5a
  • #49 Second fundamental form, principal curvatures and mean curvature of a hypersurface: R5a, R5a.2, R7.9 (flat-ambient case in code, also on manifold sources; normal bundle ν_f and the vector-valued B, S, ∇⊥ of an immersion in code)
  • #52 Minimal hypersurface in a Riemannian manifold: R5a.1 (relative volume density, the relative volume on a compact manifold and its C^k regularity on a noncompact one in code), R5a.2, R5b, R5c
  • #62 Riemannian symmetric space of a semisimple Lie group: R1.3
  • #65 Strict stability of a minimal cone/hypersurface (Simon's inequality): R5b, cones in R5a.2
  • #66 Strictly convex boundary of a Riemannian manifold: R5a, R5c, R7.9
RiemannianGeometry/MinimalSurfacesMinimal surfacesMergedDevelops the theory of smooth minimal hypersurfaces in Riemannian manifolds and of minimal surfaces in three-manifolds. Main results include the half-space theorem, ε-regularity and local compactness, the Morse index and stability, the curvature estimate for stable surfaces, removable singularities, Frankel's theorem and the Choi–Schoen compactness theorem.
  • #52 Minimal hypersurface in a Riemannian manifold: MS6.1–MS6.3 (index, nullity)
  • #65 Strict stability of a minimal cone/hypersurface (Simon's inequality): §7.8

Also cited in the evidence for #49 Second fundamental form, principal curvatures and mean curvature of a hypersurface, #66 Strictly convex boundary of a Riemannian manifold.

RiemannianManifoldsWithConvexBoundaryRiemannian manifolds with convex boundaryMergedTreats the boundary of a Riemannian manifold with boundary as a hypersurface: boundary defining functions, the second fundamental form, and strictly convex, convex and strictly concave boundaries with their characterisation by Hessians. It goes on to geodesics at the boundary, boundary normal coordinates, and the exit time and scattering relation of compact non-trapping manifolds.
  • #49 Second fundamental form, principal curvatures and mean curvature of a hypersurface: §§1.4–1.5, §2.1 (§§1.1–1.3 and the boundary II in code)
  • #66 Strictly convex boundary of a Riemannian manifold: §§1.3–1.5, 2.1–2.3, 4.2, 4.4 (II of ∂M, convexity predicates, Hessian characterisation, boundary normal coordinates)
SatoTateGroupsSato–Tate groupsMergedDevelops Sato–Tate groups as closed subgroups of unitary groups satisfying the axioms of Fité, Kedlaya, Rotger and Sutherland, with moments and event probabilities computed by Weyl integration. Main results are the classifications in degrees two and four, the latter with fifty-seven groups, the Sato–Tate group of an abelian variety built from its ℓ-adic Galois image, with fifty-two of these groups matched to endomorphism types of abelian surfaces, a general statement of the Sato–Tate conjecture, and its proof for elliptic curves with complex multiplication.

Also cited in the evidence for #12 The Hodge structure on H1 of a complex abelian variety, #13 Mumford–Tate group of a rational Hodge structure, #23 Hodge-generic abelian variety, #89 ℓ-adic Galois representation with values in the Mumford–Tate group.

SchemeAndStackFoundationsScheme, stack, cohomology and intersection foundationsMergedCollects the general algebraic geometry on which arithmetic geometry rests. It covers descent, algebraic spaces, torsors and algebraic stacks, and cohomology on the Zariski, étale, fppf and pro-étale sites with coherent duality. It also treats curves, divisors and Picard functors, deformation theory, formal schemes and alterations, and Chow groups with Chern classes and Riemann–Roch.
  • #17 Cohomologically affine morphisms of algebraic stacks: §1.22 (QCoh.pushforward)
  • #20 Families of Enriques surfaces and the moduli stack ℳEnr: §§1.2–1.9 (algebraic spaces)
  • #28 Points of an algebraic stack: §1.20 (Stacks.points, automorphismGroup)
SelmerIwasawaCohomologySelmer groups and Iwasawa cohomologyMergedBuilds Selmer groups and Selmer complexes for p-adic Galois representations, compares local conditions under Tate duality, and carries them up cyclotomic towers and towers with group ℤₚᵈ as Iwasawa cohomology, with control and duality theorems. It includes Kummer theory, Selmer groups and the Tate–Shafarevich group of abelian varieties over number fields, structure theorems for ordinary Selmer groups, and a precise statement of the Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjecture.
  • #87 Tate–Shafarevich group of an abelian variety over a number field: §2.12a (code has elliptic-curve sha, the abstract Selmer kernel SelmerData.selmerModule and compact Selmer structures TopSelmerStructure)
SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisationWeight and level optimisation of residual modular representationsMergedStarting from a modular two-dimensional residual Galois representation, builds the integral level-changing arguments that bring its level away from the coefficient prime to the full Artin conductor and its weight to the Serre weight: lowering the level away from p, weight optimisation at p, and removing the coefficient prime from the level. It includes odd-characteristic companion forms, the weight-one and Jacobian multiplicity inputs for nonregular modularity lifting, Buzzard's dyadic arguments, and the totally real optimisation theorems used inside the proof of Khare–Wintenberger. Global residual modularity is a hypothesis throughout the general optimisation results.

Also cited in the evidence for #69 Galois representation attached to a cuspidal Hecke eigenform, #71 Abelian variety Af attached to a weight-2 newform.

ShimuraCompactificationsToroidal compactifications and boundary geometryMergedConstructs toroidal compactifications of Shimura varieties and their integral models, with the boundary geometry on which automorphic forms, q-expansions and Hecke operators depend. Main results are Pink's toroidal compactification in characteristic zero with its canonical model and map to the Baily–Borel compactification, independence of coherent cohomology from the cone decomposition, degenerations of abelian schemes, integral toroidal and minimal compactifications of PEL Shimura varieties at good primes with the Koecher principle, and Dimitrov's integral compactifications of Hilbert modular varieties with the q-expansion principle.

Also cited in the evidence for #15 Ball quotients as quasi-projective varieties.

ShimuraDataShimura data, Hermitian domains, and adelic level structuresMergedBuilds the Hodge-theoretic and group-theoretic material used with Shimura data: rational weights, Mumford–Tate groups and Hodge-generic abelian varieties, variations of Hodge structure and their polarizations, compact duals over reflex fields, and explicit Hilbert and Siegel data. Its main results are that every connected Shimura datum lifts to a full datum satisfying the additional axioms, and a neatness criterion for certain adelic level subgroups.
  • #13 Mumford–Tate group of a rational Hodge structure: §§1.1–1.4
  • #23 Hodge-generic abelian variety: §1.5 hodgeGeneric, on §1.2

Also cited in the evidence for #12 The Hodge structure on H1 of a complex abelian variety, #15 Ball quotients as quasi-projective varieties, #31 Totally geodesic subvariety of a ball quotient, #89 ℓ-adic Galois representation with values in the Mumford–Tate group.

ShimuraVarietiesComplex Shimura varieties and canonical modelsMergedConstructs complex Shimura varieties as towers of arithmetic quotients of hermitian symmetric domains, their Baily–Borel compactifications, and canonical models over reflex fields: first the Siegel case by complex multiplication, then Hodge and abelian type, then general Shimura data. It also treats Mumford–Tate groups and the Galois representations of abelian varieties with values in them.
  • #13 Mumford–Tate group of a rational Hodge structure: §P.3a
  • #15 Ball quotients as quasi-projective varieties: §2.7

  • #89 ℓ-adic Galois representation with values in the Mumford–Tate group: §7.0i.1, on §§7.0i, P.3a (code: Mumford–Tate Hopf ideal mumfordTateHopfIdeal)

Also cited in the evidence for #31 Totally geodesic subvariety of a ball quotient, #74 Complex CM periods of a CM abelian scheme.

SmallRamificationAndAbelianVarietyBaseCasesSmall ramification and the base cases for Serre modularityMergedDevelops the arithmetic results that end the level-one arguments for Serre modularity: bounds turning a small local different into a small global discriminant, and the Tate–Serre base cases in characteristics two and three, where finite subgroup theory rules out an absolutely irreducible representation. For abelian varieties it determines the simple factors and extensions of finite flat torsion and compares growing torsion with a fixed finite-field point count, giving Fontaine's theorem and Schoof's five-prime theorem as geometric base cases. A realisation theorem for weight-two representations brings these back to residual representations and gives a table of terminal weights.

Also cited in the evidence for #71 Abelian variety Af attached to a weight-2 newform.

SmoothRepresentationsOfLocalGroupsSmooth representations of local groupsMergedBuilds the smooth representation theory of locally profinite groups over a general coefficient ring, and of reductive p-adic groups in particular: Hecke algebras, parabolic induction and Jacquet functors, admissibility, second adjointness, the Bernstein decomposition and centre, and the Satake isomorphism. These are the local representations and Hecke algebras that the global automorphic theory uses.
  • #67 Local components of an automorphic representation (restricted tensor product)
  • #73 Central character of an automorphic representation: SR.3a.1
  • #86 Symmetric power of a Weil–Deligne representation: SR.5.2

Also cited in the evidence for #72 CM automorphic representation, #83 Schwartz (Hecke) algebra of an adelic reductive group, #88 Weak functorial transfer to GLN, #90 The π-nearly isotypic cuspidal subspace.

SobolevDifferentialFormsSobolev spaces of differential forms and the L² de Rham complexMergedBuilds the Sobolev theory of differential forms on Euclidean domains: the weak exterior derivative, the spaces of Lᵖ forms whose weak exterior derivative is in Lᵖ for 1 ≤ p ≤ ∞, pullback under diffeomorphisms and bi-Lipschitz maps, tangential traces, Green's formula and gluing of forms along common boundaries. Main results are the L² de Rham complex as a Hilbert complex with its Hodge decomposition, the Gaffney identity on the cube, and Picard's compactness theorem with closed range of the complex on bounded Lipschitz domains.

Also cited in the evidence for #41 Quasiregular map between Riemannian manifolds, #42 Quasiregularly elliptic manifold, #47 Sobolev space W1,ploc(M,N) of maps between Riemannian manifolds.

SobolevMapsBetweenManifoldsSobolev maps between manifoldsMergedDevelops first-order Sobolev maps between manifolds: the local class of continuous maps whose chart representatives lie locally in the Sobolev space of exponent p, the weak differential, its norm and, for oriented Riemannian manifolds of equal dimension, the Jacobian. Main results are independence of charts and metrics, chain rules, pullback of differential forms commuting with the exterior derivative, smooth approximation, and a degree for Sobolev maps of exponent n between closed oriented manifolds of dimension n.
  • #41 Quasiregular map between Riemannian manifolds: §§4.2–4.3, 5.1, 6.1–6.3
  • #42 Quasiregularly elliptic manifold
  • #47 Sobolev space W1,ploc(M,N) of maps between Riemannian manifolds: §4.2 IsSobolevMap, §4.3 HasWeakMFDeriv, §5.1, §6.2, §9.1 (§6.1 and the a.e. filter in code)
SpectralSequencesAndConvergenceSpectral sequences and convergenceMergedBuilds, over an arbitrary abelian category, the language in which a spectral sequence converges: cycle and boundary towers, the limit page, and weak, ordinary and strong convergence to filtered objects in a prescribed set of degrees. It proves the comparison theorem, convergence for filtered complexes under the usual boundedness hypotheses, and Boardman's theorems on conditional convergence of exact couples.
  • #6 Convergence of a spectral sequence: §§1.4, 3.2, 3.4, 4.4
SpectralSequencesAndConvergencePartIISpectral sequences and convergence, part II — the standard constructions and their productsMergedConstructs the standard spectral sequences of homological algebra and proves that they converge: the two spectral sequences of a double complex, the hypercohomology and composite-functor spectral sequences, the universal-coefficient, Künneth and change-of-rings spectral sequences, and the Bockstein spectral sequence. It also treats products, with the Leibniz rule on every page.

Also cited in the evidence for #6 Convergence of a spectral sequence.

StableReductionStable reduction of curves and stable mapsMergedProves stable reduction for curves over a discrete valuation ring: after a finite extension of the base, a stable model exists and is unique. It also defines stable maps to a scheme and their families, with the stability criteria and the stabilization construction that a proof of properness of the moduli space of stable maps requires; the moduli stack itself is not constructed here.
  • #24 Moduli stack of stable maps ℳg,n(X,β): L10–11 (single stable maps; IsStableMap in code)
StableReductionPartIIStable reduction of curves, Part IIMergedDevelops, building on stable reduction over discrete valuation rings, the explicit geometry of moduli of stable pointed curves over arbitrary bases: tricanonical embeddings, local charts at nodes, adding and contracting markings, Hodge bundles, stable compactifications, Torelli maps and relative Picard functors. Main results identify the universal curve with the moduli of curves with one extra marking, give the integral Noether isomorphism of determinant lines, extend families after finite base change, and prove positivity of the Hodge bundle under maximal variation.
  • #24 Moduli stack of stable maps ℳg,n(X,β): §§3.1–3.3, 3.11
StackPointsAndResidualGerbesPoints of algebraic stacks, stabilisers and residual gerbesMergedDevelops the local structure of an algebraic stack at a point, in the conventions of the Stacks Project: substacks, finite type and closed points, automorphism groups, gerbes, and the residual gerbe at a point with its existence theorems. Its main results identify the residual gerbe at a rational point with the classifying stack of the automorphism group, and relate the tangent space to the normal space at a closed rational point.
  • #28 Points of an algebraic stack: §§0.3, 1.1–1.2
  • #29 Residual gerbe at a point of an algebraic stack: §§3.3, 4.1–4.5, 6.3–6.4, 7.2 (Stacks.residualGerbe, 𝒵_x ≃ BG_x, N_x)

Also cited in the evidence for #16 Rational Chow groups of a Deligne–Mumford stack and the degree of zero-cycles, #17 Cohomologically affine morphisms of algebraic stacks.

SymplecticGeometry/CotangentLagrangianCotangent bundles and Lagrangian geometryMergedDevelops the symplectic geometry of cotangent bundles and Lagrangian submanifolds: the cotangent bundle as a phase space, embedded Lagrangians, generating functions and the canonical transformations they define, and neighbourhood theorems for symplectic germs along a submanifold. Convex planar billiards appear as an example of a map given by a generating function.
  • #92 Billiard ball map of a convex billiard table: C3.1
TaubesGromovTaubes–Gromov theoryMergedConstructs Taubes's integer count of embedded pseudoholomorphic curves in a closed symplectic four-manifold and relates it to the Seiberg–Witten invariant through vortices and the monopole equations with a large parameter. It includes the normal operators of embedded curves and an application to mapping tori of surfaces.
  • #60 Normal Cauchy–Riemann operator of a J-holomorphic curve: §1.2
TeichmullerGeometryTeichmüller geometry of closed surfacesMergedBuilds the Teichmüller theory of a closed oriented surface of genus at least two: the space of marked complex structures with its complex structure of dimension 3g − 3, the action of the mapping class group and the Dehn–Nielsen–Baer theorem, Fenchel–Nielsen coordinates, the Teichmüller metric, and the Nielsen realization theorem. Its final results are that the marked hyperbolic surfaces with trivial isometry group, those with nonarithmetic uniformizing group, and those whose uniformizing group is maximal among discrete subgroups of PGL₂(ℝ) each form an open dense subset of Teichmüller space.
  • #59 Mapping class group of a punctured surface: §0.0h (MappingClassGroupFull, in code)
  • #61 Outer action of the mapping class group on the fundamental group: §§4.2, 5.4

Also cited in the evidence for #54 Character variety of a surface group in a real reductive group, #58 MCG-finite representation of a surface group, #68 Arithmetic lattice in a semisimple Lie group, #93 Billiard flow in a polygon.

TracesOnCStarAlgebrasTraces on C*-algebras and their trace idealsMergedDevelops possibly unbounded traces on C*-algebras in Dixmier's sense, with their domain ideals, lower semicontinuity, semifiniteness, and the trace ideals of elements b with |b|ᵖ of finite trace. Main results are Combes's theorem, Dixmier's theorem extending a semifinite lower semicontinuous trace to a faithful normal semifinite trace on the generated von Neumann algebra, Hölder inequalities for trace ideals, invariance of K-theory under passage to trace ideals, and, for AF algebras, a bijection between densely defined lower semicontinuous traces and positive homomorphisms from K₀ to ℝ.

Also cited in the evidence for #36 Amenable trace on a C*-algebra.

VariationalMethodsVariational methods and constrained spectral theoryMergedDevelops the link between weak compactness in Banach spaces, minimization of quadratic energies and self-adjoint operators on Hilbert space, for a form domain compactly and densely embedded in a Hilbert space. Main results are the direct method of the calculus of variations, constrained Rayleigh minimizers with their Euler–Lagrange equation, an orthonormal eigenbasis with eigenvalues tending to infinity, the Courant–Fischer min–max principle on the form domain, compact resolvent and spectrum of the associated operator, and convergence of Ritz–Galerkin approximations.

Also cited in the evidence for #34 Neumann eigenvalues and eigenfunctions of the Laplacian on a bounded domain.

VarifoldsVarifoldsMergedBuilds the theory of k-dimensional varifolds on open subsets of a Riemannian manifold, in the generality of Allard: Radon measures on the Grassmann bundle, their first variation, and stationary, rectifiable and integral varifolds. Main results are the monotonicity formula, Allard's rectifiability theorem, the compactness theorems for rectifiable and integral varifolds, tangent cones and the constancy theorem.

  • #65 Strict stability of a minimal cone/hypersurface (Simon's inequality): §§2.3, 4.3, 9.1

VirtualFundamentalClassThe Behrend–Fantechi virtual fundamental classMergedCarries out the construction of Behrend and Fantechi: the intrinsic normal cone of a Deligne–Mumford stack, obstruction theories, and the virtual fundamental class of a perfect obstruction theory with a global resolution, shown to be independent of all choices. Along the way it builds rational Chow groups, Gysin maps and degrees of zero-cycles for separated Deligne–Mumford stacks, and a relative version of the class.
  • #16 Rational Chow groups of a Deligne–Mumford stack and the degree of zero-cycles: §§2.1–2.2, 7.7 (rational Chow groups of separated DM stacks, degree on proper stacks)
  • #27 Perfect obstruction theory and virtual fundamental class: Layers 4–7, §§9.1–9.2
VonNeumannAlgebrasVon Neumann algebras, factors, and modular theoryMergedDevelops the theory of von Neumann algebras: operator topologies, preduals and normality, projection comparison, standard forms, Hilbert modules with coupling constant and index, group algebras and crossed products, Tomita–Takesaki theory, weights, Connes's invariants and ultraproducts. Main results are the bicommutant and Kaplansky density theorems, existence and uniqueness of the trace on a II₁ factor, the Tomita–Takesaki theorem, Connes's classification of type III factors, Dye's theorem, and the equivalence of injectivity with hyperfiniteness together with the classification of injective factors.

Also cited in the evidence for #36 Amenable trace on a C*-algebra, #46 Schur multiplier on Schatten classes.

WeightsInEtaleCohomologyWeights in étale cohomology: arithmetic cohomology and eigenform applicationsMergedConnects weight theorems to the arithmetic representations that occur in good reduction, modular forms and local comparison theorems, building the comparisons that identify Frobenius on a representation with Frobenius on a geometric fibre and fixing the coefficient and Tate-twist conventions. It treats good reduction of curves and abelian varieties, specialization and degeneration models with Picard–Lefschetz normalization, pencils over finite fields, and arithmetic cohomology with projectors, including parabolic purity and hard Lefschetz. The applications to eigenforms are Ramanujan bounds and the local polynomials of a fixed form at good primes.

Also cited in the evidence for #69 Galois representation attached to a cuspidal Hecke eigenform, #71 Abelian variety Af attached to a weight-2 newform.

WeilConjecturesWeil conjectures and cohomological zeta functionsMergedRelates point counts over finite fields to the zeta function and its cohomological factors, and assembles the Weil conjectures for a smooth proper scheme over a finite field: integral factors independent of ℓ, the absolute values of the reciprocal roots, and the functional equation. It also contains explicit calculations for curves and surfaces, among them the definition and numerical invariants of Enriques surfaces.
  • #19 Enriques surface over an algebraically closed field: §7.12 (Enriques condition over any field)
  • #20 Families of Enriques surfaces and the moduli stack ℳEnr: §7.12
YangMillsConnectionsAndCaloricGaugeYang–Mills connections on Euclidean space and the caloric gaugeMergedDevelops connections on Euclidean space with values in a compact Lie group at critical regularity: Sobolev gauge transformations, Uhlenbeck's Coulomb gauge, the good global gauge theorem, and the topological class and instanton number of a finite-energy connection. It then treats harmonic Yang–Mills connections on ℝ⁴ (energy gap and ground state), the Yang–Mills heat flow, and the caloric gauge of Oh and Tataru, ending with the theorem that the caloric connections form a C¹ submanifold of a Banach space.
  • #37 Caloric gauge and the manifold of caloric connections: Layer 9, §§10.1–10.5
  • #48 Topological class (instanton number) of a finite-energy connection on ℝ4: §§3.1–3.2, §§5.2–5.5, §6.4

Roadmaps in open PRs (51)

RoadmapWhat it containsDefinitions
AbelianSurfacesModularityModularity of abelian surfacesOpen PR #1746Develops the 2–3 switch for abelian surfaces over ℚ: permitted mod-3 symplectic images, ordinary local points at 2 and 3 on moduli of marked genus-two curves, and residual automorphy of mod-2 representations with image A₅ or S₅. With ordinary modularity lifting for GSp₄ as input, it proves modularity of abelian surfaces and genus-two curves over ℚ with good ordinary reduction at 3, a permitted mod-3 image and conditions at 2, hence entire L-functions with a functional equation, a positive density of modular genus-two equations, and residual modularity modulo 2.

Also cited in the evidence for #30 Theta divisor of a Jacobian.

AbundanceStatementStating abundance for projective klt varietiesOpen PR #545Develops the divisor, positivity and singularity theory needed to state the abundance conjecture: for a normal projective variety with klt singularities over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, a nef canonical divisor is semiample. It builds ℚ-Cartier divisors on normal varieties, the canonical divisor from differentials, discrepancies and klt singularities, the cone of curves and nefness, ampleness with Kleiman's criterion, and semiampleness, and formulates the conjecture as a precise statement without proving it.

Also cited in the evidence for #95 Dynamical degree of a dominant rational self-map.

AlgebraicGromovWittenTheoryAlgebraic Gromov–Witten theory and quantum cohomologyOpen PR #1998Constructs algebraic Gromov–Witten theory for a smooth projective variety over a field of characteristic zero: the moduli stack of stable maps, its virtual fundamental class, and primary and descendant invariants with the string, dilaton and divisor equations. From the genus-zero invariants it builds small and big quantum cohomology and proves associativity through the WDVV equation.
  • #24 Moduli stack of stable maps ℳg,n(X,β): Layer 3 MbarMap
  • #27 Perfect obstruction theory and virtual fundamental class: Layer 4

Also cited in the evidence for #16 Rational Chow groups of a Deligne–Mumford stack and the degree of zero-cycles.

AutomorphicCongruencesAutomorphic congruences and reverse main-conjecture divisibilitiesOpen PR #1816Develops Eisenstein congruences on unitary groups that produce Galois extension classes and lower bounds for Selmer groups, with the comparisons of periods and local conditions and the passage to universal deformation families and cyclotomic towers. Main results are the Skinner–Urban divisibilities, the Castella–Liu–Wan and Fouquet–Wan divisibilities, Wan's divisibility for Hilbert families, and the Burungale–Castella–Skinner cyclotomic main conjecture for elliptic curves over ℚ with good ordinary reduction at p > 3, after inverting p and, under an image condition, integrally, each under its own hypotheses.

Also cited in the evidence for #75 Critical infinity types CritL(Γ) and the attached CM type.

AutomorphicSpectralTheoryPartIIAutomorphic spectral theory and trace distributions, part IIOpen PR #1761Develops multipliers of the Schwartz algebra of a reductive group over a number field: entire functions of controlled growth give multipliers on the real group, which are combined with spherical Hecke operators at finite places. The main results use such multipliers to isolate prescribed parts of the cuspidal spectrum, with the supporting analysis of weighted function spaces on automorphic quotients.
  • #83 Schwartz (Hecke) algebra of an adelic reductive group: §§1.1, 1.9, 4.3
  • #90 The π-nearly isotypic cuspidal subspace: §4.2a

CompressibleEulerEquationsPartIICompressible Euler equations, part II: acoustical geometry and characteristic dataOpen PR #3010Builds the geometry of the acoustical metric of a solution of the compressible Euler equations, whose null cones are the sound cones: acoustical functions whose level sets are sound fronts, acoustical coordinates, and the characteristic boundary of a development, each point of which lies on a null geodesic issuing from the edge of the data. It then treats initial data prescribed partly on a characteristic hypersurface, with their developments, maximal developments, local existence near the edge and the Goursat problem, and shows that the plane-symmetric centered rarefaction wave of a polytropic gas fits this setting.

GlaubermanZStarIsolated involutions and Glauberman's Z* theoremOpen PR #1778Proves Glauberman's Z* theorem: in a finite group, an involution that commutes with none of its other conjugates is central modulo the largest normal subgroup of odd order. It develops isolated involutions and their fusion, the reduction of a minimal counterexample, the Brauer–Suzuki theorem for groups with generalized quaternion Sylow 2-subgroups, constancy of principal 2-block characters on involution sections, and the character-value argument that completes the proof.

GrossZagierAndArithmeticHeightsPartIIGross–Zagier formulas and arithmetic heights, part II — unitary special cycles and the arithmetic fundamental lemmaOpen PR #2457Builds the arithmetic intersection theory of special cycles on unitary Rapoport–Zink spaces and unitary Shimura varieties of signature (n−1,1). Main results are the Kudla–Rapoport identities in four unramified and ramified local settings, the arithmetic Siegel–Weil formulas, and Zhang's arithmetic fundamental lemma for strongly regular elements when p is large relative to n. In the ramified Krämer setting the identity is proved in rank at most three and stated in higher rank under an explicit hypothesis, as is the ramified Siegel–Weil formula in dimension at least four.

It builds the group of C^{k,α} diffeomorphisms with its inverse function theorems and topologies, showing that the C^{k,α} topology does not make it a topological group while the topology of convergence in every lower Hölder exponent does. IgusaVarietiesAndTorsionConcentrationPartIIIgusa varieties and torsion concentration, Part II: compact unitary Shimura varietiesOpen PR #2439Proves the Caraiani–Scholze theorem on the generic part of the mod ℓ cohomology of compact unitary PEL Shimura varieties, both Kottwitz's simple Shimura varieties and those attached to hermitian spaces quasi-split at all finite places. LocalAnabelianGeometryPartIILocal anabelian geometry, Part II: Abrashkin's theoremsOpen PR #2867Proves Abrashkin's theorems that a nonarchimedean local field of residue characteristic p, of characteristic 0 or p, is determined by the maximal pro-p quotient of its absolute Galois group together with its upper ramification filtration. SymmetricHyperbolicSystemsLinear symmetric hyperbolic systemsOpen PR #2246Develops the classical theory of linear first-order systems on ℝ × ℝⁿ that are symmetric hyperbolic in the sense of Friedrichs, by Friedrichs' energy method and his finite difference scheme, without Fourier analysis or Sobolev spaces. For smooth coefficients with bounded derivatives and smooth compactly supported data, main results are existence of a C¹ solution of the Cauchy problem, uniqueness with a domain of dependence and explicit propagation speed, the L² energy estimate, and convergence of the scheme under the Courant–Friedrichs–Lewy condition.
  • #33 Domain of dependence and development of initial data for the compressible Euler equations
TwistedAutomorphicDescentsTwisted automorphic descents and cuspidal automorphic modulesOpen PR #1758Develops the twisted automorphic descent of Jiang and Zhang for special orthogonal and unitary groups over number fields: Bessel–Fourier coefficients of leading residues of Eisenstein series, unfolding of Bessel–Eisenstein integrals, and their factorization into local integrals. Main results are that a nonzero Bessel period forces a nonzero central L-value, the converse under a weak Fourier support hypothesis, and recovery of a prescribed cuspidal automorphic representation, up to outer automorphism, from regular or subregular Fourier support, with conjectural inputs kept as explicit hypotheses.

Also cited in the evidence for #11 Split classical groups Sp2n, SOl and general spin groups GSpinl.

VirtualFundamentalClassPartIIVirtual fundamental classes, part II: integral and non-separated Chow theory of Deligne–Mumford stacks and virtual classes over general basesOpen PR #2446Extends the Chow theory of Deligne–Mumford stacks to integral coefficients and to stacks that need not be separated, with the intersection ring of a smooth stack, the comparison with the moduli space, and the degree of the residual gerbe at a closed point. It defines the virtual class without the resolution property, proves deformation invariance of virtual counts, and constructs the perfect obstruction theory of spaces of maps from curves.
  • #16 Rational Chow groups of a Deligne–Mumford stack and the degree of zero-cycles: §§1.0, 2.2, 5.2, 5.4
  • #27 Perfect obstruction theory and virtual fundamental class: Layer 9 (ownership unresolved); §6.2

Also cited in the evidence for #24 Moduli stack of stable maps ℳg,n(X,β), #29 Residual gerbe at a point of an algebraic stack.
